1621:     boundary are exactly what an attestation read wants to see).
1622:     """
1623:     import argparse
1624:     from painted import run_cli, OutputMode
1625:     from painted.cli import HelpArg
1626: 
1627:     pre = argparse.ArgumentParser(add_help=False)
1628:     if vertex_path is None:
1629:         pre.add_argument("file", nargs="?", default=None)
1630:     pre.add_argument("--chain", action="store_true", default=False)
1631:     pre.add_argument("--since", default=None)
1632:     known, rest = pre.parse_known_args(argv)
1633:     file_arg = getattr(known, "file", None)
1634: 
1635:     target_path = _resolve_target(file_arg, vertex_path).resolve()
1636: 
1637:     help_args = (
1638:         [HelpArg("file", "Vertex .vertex file or vertex name", positional=True)]
1639:         if vertex_path is None else []
1640:     )
1641:     help_args += [
1642:         HelpArg("--chain", "Project the attestation envelope (chain linkage, "
1643:                 "signature, window cursor) and span the full hash chain"),
1644:         HelpArg("--since", "Narrow to ticks within a window (e.g. 7d, 24h); "
1645:                 "default is the full chain"),
1646:     ]
1647: 
1648:     def fetch():
1649:         import dataclasses
1650:         from lang import parse_vertex_file
1651: 
1652:         from .fetch import fetch_tick_windows
1653: 
1654:         # Validation is deferred into fetch (mirroring store stats/verify) so
1655:         # `store ticks --help` reaches run_cli's help handler — an eager guard
1656:         # would raise on target resolution before help could render.
1657:         if target_path.suffix != ".vertex":
1658:             raise ValueError(
1659:                 "store ticks requires a .vertex target — the tick series "
1660:                 "resolves through the vertex name and store"
1661:             )
1662:         # Refuse aggregates: a combine/discover vertex has no own store and no
1663:         # own chain — vertex_ticks returns empty attestation envelopes for it,
1664:         # which would render a genuinely-signed chain as a false "all legacy".
1665:         # The sibling store verbs already refuse storeless aggregates (via
1666:         # resolve_store_path); ticks bypasses that path, so guard here.
1667:         ast = parse_vertex_file(target_path)
1668:         if ast.combine is not None or ast.discover is not None:
1669:             raise ValueError(
1670:                 "store ticks reads one store's attestation chain; "
1671:                 f"{target_path.name} is an aggregate vertex (no own chain) — "
1672:                 "point at the instance store, e.g. .loops/<name>.vertex"
1673:             )
1674: 
1675:         # State 2: present .vertex / absent .db is "not yet materialized" —
1676:         # RC=1 with a surfaced message, matching stats/verify, not ticks'
1677:         # prior silent RC=0-empty. (decision/design/
1678:         # store-verb-existence-exit-code-parity)
1679:         # The gate runs FIRST: _require_materialized_store resolves through
1680:         # ensure_index, which repairs a stale index — and a repaired index
1681:         # agrees with the log by construction. Judge, then resolve.
1682:         _gate_read(target_path)
1683:         _require_materialized_store(target_path)
1684: 
1685:         # --chain spans the full hash chain (all_names) to agree with
1686:         # `store verify`/`store stats`; density stays name-scoped (its
1687:         # delta fields are a same-series concept).
1688:         windows = fetch_tick_windows(
1689:             target_path, since=known.since, all_names=known.chain
1690:         )
1691:         chain = {
1692:             "ticks": len(windows),
1693:             "chained": sum(1 for w in windows if w.chained),
1694:             "signed": sum(1 for w in windows if w.signed),
1695:             "legacy": sum(1 for w in windows if not w.chained),
1696:         }
1697:         window_dicts = [dataclasses.asdict(w) for w in windows]
1698: 
1699:         # Density default projects each tick as a sealed window of attention:
1700:         # window-scoped fact count + kind mix + MAX tier over touched keys
1701:         # (the TickWindow fields are cumulative fold state; these are the
1702:         # per-window complements). Skipped under --chain — the attestation
1703:         # projection reads the stored envelope only.
1704:         if not known.chain:
1705:             from .fetch import stamp_window_stats
1706: 
1707:             stamp_window_stats(target_path, window_dicts)
1708: 
1709:         return {
1710:             "vertex": ast.name,
1711:             "chain_mode": known.chain,
1712:             "chain": chain,
1713:             "since": known.since,
1714:             "windows": window_dicts,
1715:         }
1716: 
1717:     def renderer(data, fidelity, width):
1718:         from ..lenses.store import tick_chain_view
1719:         from loops.lens_resolver import zoom_from_fidelity
1720:         return tick_chain_view(data, zoom_from_fidelity(fidelity), width)
1721: 
1722:     return run_cli(
1723:         rest,
1724:         fetch=fetch,
1725:         renderer=renderer,
1726:         default_mode=OutputMode.STATIC,
1727:         prog="loops store ticks",
1728:         description=(
1729:             "Read a store's tick series. The default projects each tick as "
1730:             "a sealed window of attention — window fact count, kind mix, "
1731:             "span, rail tier (-v adds the touched keys); --chain projects "
1732:             "the attestation envelope (chain linkage, signature, window "
1733:             "cursor) per tick."
1734:         ),
1735:         help_args=help_args,
1736:     )
1737: 
1738: 
1739: def _run_store_stats(argv: list[str], *, vertex_path: Path | None = None) -> int:
1740:     """Store statistics — topline totals and (``--by-kind``) a
1741:     count-descending per-kind tally. Works on a ``.db`` or a ``.vertex``.
1742:     """
1743:     import argparse
1744:     from painted import run_cli, OutputMode
1745:     from painted.cli import HelpArg
1746: 
1747:     pre = argparse.ArgumentParser(add_help=False)
1748:     if vertex_path is None:
1749:         pre.add_argument("file", nargs="?", default=None)
1750:     pre.add_argument("--by-kind", dest="by_kind", action="store_true", default=False)
1751:     pre.add_argument(
1752:         "--kind", default=None,
1753:         help="Narrow to one kind (escape hatch for the reserved _decl.* namespace)",
1754:     )
1755:     known, rest = pre.parse_known_args(argv)
1756:     file_arg = getattr(known, "file", None)
1757: 
1758:     target_path = _resolve_target(file_arg, vertex_path).resolve()
1759: 
1760:     help_args = (
1761:         [HelpArg("file", "Store .db or .vertex file, or vertex name",
1762:                  positional=True)]
1763:         if vertex_path is None else []
1764:     )
1765: 
1766:     def fetch():
1767:         from engine.store_reader import StoreReader
1768: 
1769:         _gate_read(target_path)
1770:         store_path = _require_materialized_store(target_path)
1771:         with StoreReader(store_path) as reader:
1772:             # Explicit --kind is the SPEC §9.4 escape hatch — it overrides
1773:             # the ambient _decl.* exclusion, same rule as `loops store` base.
1774:             summary = reader.summary(include_internal=known.kind is not None)
1775:         if known.kind is not None:
1776:             summary["facts"]["kinds"] = {
1777:                 k: v for k, v in summary["facts"]["kinds"].items() if k == known.kind
1778:             }
1779:         kinds = sorted(
1780:             (
1781:                 {"kind": k, "count": v["count"]}
1782:                 for k, v in summary["facts"]["kinds"].items()
1783:             ),
1784:             key=lambda r: r["count"],
1785:             reverse=True,
1786:         )
1787:         return {
1788:             "vertex": target_path.stem,
1789:             "by_kind": known.by_kind,
1790:             "total_facts": summary["facts"]["total"],
1791:             "total_ticks": summary["ticks"]["total"],
1792:             "kind_count": len(kinds),
1793:             "kinds": kinds,
1794:         }
1795: 
1796:     def renderer(data, fidelity, width):
1797:         from ..lenses.store import stats_view
1798:         from loops.lens_resolver import zoom_from_fidelity
1799:         return stats_view(data, zoom_from_fidelity(fidelity), width)
1800: 
1801:     return run_cli(
1802:         rest,
1803:         fetch=fetch,
1804:         renderer=renderer,
1805:         default_mode=OutputMode.STATIC,
1806:         prog="loops store stats",
1807:         description=(
1808:             "Store statistics. --by-kind adds a count-descending per-kind tally."
1809:         ),
1810:         help_args=help_args,
1811:     )
1812: 
1813: 
1814: def _run_reindex(argv: list[str], *, vertex_path: Path | None = None) -> int:
1815:     """``sl store reindex <vertex>`` — drop and rebuild the FTS derived index.
1816: 
1817:     The SOLE writer of ``facts_fts``/``fts_state`` anywhere in this codebase
1818:     (closes friction:search-read-mutates-canonical-store): every read path
1819:     (``vertex_search``, and its aggregation recursion) is strictly
1820:     read-only, so this verb is now the only supported way to build or
1821:     refresh the search index. Because it's a full drop+rebuild against the
1822:     vertex's CURRENT ``search`` declarations rather than an incremental
1823:     watermark, it also closes friction:fts-search-declaration-not-retroactive
1824:     for free — a newly-declared ``search`` field on an already-populated
1825:     kind is fully searchable after one reindex, including facts written
1826:     before the declaration existed.
1827: 
1828:     Unlike ``ticks``/``absorb`` (which require a single store and refuse
1829:     combine/discover aggregates outright), reindex does NOT refuse an
1830:     aggregate — it recurses per child, mirroring the read-side recursion
1831:     ``vertex_search``/``vertex_search_coverage`` already do, so reindexing
1832:     an aggregate touches only each child's own store and nothing else.
1833: 
1834:     For a single (non-aggregate) target, the store must already be
1835:     materialized — same existence contract as ticks/stats/verify
1836:     (decision/design/store-verb-existence-exit-code-parity): a present
1837:     ``.vertex`` whose store was never written refuses cleanly rather than
1838:     silently no-op'ing, so an operator asking to reindex a specific target
1839:     gets a clear answer. (The underlying ``engine.vertex_reindex`` primitive
1840:     is more lenient about a missing store — it no-ops instead of raising —
1841:     because it doubles as the per-child recursion step for an aggregate,
1842:     where a not-yet-materialized child must not abort the whole reindex.)
1843:     """
1844:     import argparse
1845: 
1846:     p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
1847:         prog="loops store reindex",
1848:         description="Drop and rebuild the FTS5 derived index (facts_fts/"
1849:                     "fts_state) against the vertex's current search "
1850:                     "declarations. The sole writer of the FTS index — every "
1851:                     "read path stays strictly read-only.",
1852:     )
1853:     if vertex_path is None:
1854:         p.add_argument("file", nargs="?", help="Vertex .vertex file or vertex name")
1855:     p.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="JSON receipt")
1856:     # -h/--help is owned by argparse (add_help=True): parse_args prints the
1857:     # help built from this parser and exits 0 natively. No hand-rolled block.
1858:     args = p.parse_args(argv)
1859: 
1860:     target_path = _resolve_target(getattr(args, "file", None), vertex_path).resolve()
1861:     if target_path.suffix != ".vertex":
1862:         raise ValueError(
1863:             "reindex requires a .vertex target — search field declarations "
1864:             "live in the vertex file, not the raw database"
1865:         )
1866: 
1867:     from lang import parse_vertex_file
1868: 
1869:     ast = parse_vertex_file(target_path)
1870:     is_aggregate = ast.combine is not None or ast.discover is not None
1871:     if not is_aggregate:
1872:         # State 2 of the existence contract: a present .vertex with no
1873:         # materialized store refuses cleanly (RC=1), matching
1874:         # ticks/stats/verify — not silently no-op'd. Aggregates skip this:
1875:         # they own no store of their own, and per-child materialization is
1876:         # engine.vertex_reindex's concern (a not-yet-emitted child is a
1877:         # graceful no-op there, not a whole-aggregate refusal).
1878:         _require_materialized_store(target_path)
1879: 
1880:     from engine import vertex_reindex
1881: 
1882:     receipt = vertex_reindex(target_path)
1883: 
1884:     if args.json:
1885:         import json as _json
1886:         print(_json.dumps(receipt, indent=2))  # noqa: T201 — machine output path
1887:         return 0
1888: 
1889:     from painted import Block, Style, paint
1890: 
1891:     if receipt.get("aggregate"):
1892:         children = receipt.get("children", [])
1893:         reindexed = sum(1 for c in children if c.get("reindexed"))
1894:         line = (
1895:             f"✓ {target_path.stem}: reindexed {reindexed}/{len(children)} "
1896:             "child store(s)"
1897:         )
1898:     elif not receipt.get("reindexed"):
1899:         line = f"· {target_path.stem}: nothing to reindex — {receipt.get('reason')}"
1900:     else:
1901:         line = (
1902:             f"✓ {target_path.stem}: reindexed — "
1903:             f"{receipt['facts_indexed']}/{receipt['facts_scanned']} facts "
1904:             f"indexed across {len(receipt['kinds_indexed'])} kind(s)"
1905:         )
1906:     paint(Block.text(line, Style()))
1907:     return 0
1908: 
1909: 
1910: def _run_store(
1911:     argv: list[str], *, vertex_path: Path | None = None, observer: str | None = None
1912: ) -> int:
1913:     """Dispatch a store subcommand, converting a canonical-store refusal into
1914:     a rendered refusal rather than a traceback.
1915: 
1916:     The refusals the subcommands raise themselves are caught locally, where
1917:     the message can be specific. This is the backstop for the ones raised by
1918:     the ``JsonlStore`` **constructor** — a derived index holding rows the log
1919:     never saw makes every open refuse, including the read-shaped
1920:     verify/stats/ticks/adopt paths that have no local catch because they do
1921:     not mutate history. Those printed a raw traceback; they now say what is
1922:     wrong with the store.
1923:     """
1924:     from engine.jsonl_store import JsonlCanonicalUnsupported
1925: 
1926:     try:
1927:         return _dispatch_store(argv, vertex_path=vertex_path, observer=observer)
1928:     except JsonlCanonicalUnsupported as exc:
1929:         return _refuse_store(str(exc))
1930: 
1931: 
1932: def _dispatch_store(
1933:     argv: list[str], *, vertex_path: Path | None = None, observer: str | None = None
1934: ) -> int:
1935:     """Run store command via painted CLI harness.
1936: 
1937:     ``observer`` is the invocation-level identity (the global ``--observer``
1938:     peel) — threaded only into ``absorb``, which signs a genesis under a
1939:     recording observer; every other subcommand is observer-agnostic.
1940:     """
1941:     import argparse
1942:     from painted import run_cli, OutputMode
1943:     from painted.cli import HelpArg
1944: 
1945:     if argv and argv[0] == "verify":
1946:         return _run_verify(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1947:     if argv and argv[0] == "rebirth":
1948:         return _run_rebirth(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1949:     if argv and argv[0] == "migrate":
1950:         return _run_migrate(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1951:     if argv and argv[0] == "reanchor":
1952:         return _run_reanchor(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1953:     if argv and argv[0] == "export":
1954:         return _run_export(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1955:     if argv and argv[0] == "absorb":
1956:         return _run_absorb(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path, observer=observer)
1957:     if argv and argv[0] == "adopt":
1958:         return _run_adopt(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1959:     if argv and argv[0] == "ticks":
1960:         return _run_store_ticks(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1961:     if argv and argv[0] == "stats":
1962:         return _run_store_stats(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1963:     if argv and argv[0] == "reindex":
1964:         return _run_reindex(argv[1:], vertex_path=vertex_path)
1965: 
1966:     # Base inspect: pre-parse the optional ``file`` target; run_cli owns -h
1967:     # and the -i/-q/-v/--json/--plain axes, listing ``file`` (when accepted)
1968:     # via help_args (decision:design/devtools-help-args-idiom).
1969:     pre = argparse.ArgumentParser(add_help=False)
1970:     if vertex_path is None:
1971:         pre.add_argument("file", nargs="?", default=None)
1972:     pre.add_argument(
1973:         "--kind", default=None,
1974:         help="Narrow to one kind (escape hatch for the reserved _decl.* namespace)",
1975:     )
1976:     known, rest = pre.parse_known_args(argv)
1977:     file_arg = getattr(known, "file", None)
1978: 
1979:     # Pre-flight the empty-home case (finding:chw-r2-sibling-store-error-to-
1980:     # stdout): bare `loops store` with no config root resolves to
1981:     # `<home>/.vertex` inside fetch(), whose FileNotFoundError painted's
1982:     # run_cli prints to STDOUT by framework design. Refuse here instead —
1983:     # byte-identical message, stderr, exit 1 — mirroring the R2 ls fix
1984:     # (commands/population.py, finding:chw-r2-bare-ls-error-to-stdout). No
1985:     # local-layer clause: unlike ls, _resolve_target's bare path has no
1986:     # local-vertex fallback, so the root's absence alone decides.
1987:     if vertex_path is None and file_arg is None:
1988:         from .resolve import _err, loops_home, missing_root_message
1989: 
1990:         root_path = loops_home() / ".vertex"
1991:         if not root_path.exists():
1992:             _err(missing_root_message(root_path))
1993:             return 1
1994: 
1995:     help_args = (
1996:         [HelpArg("file", "Store .db or .vertex file, or vertex name",
1997:                  positional=True)]
1998:         if vertex_path is None else []
1999:     )
2000: 
2001:     def _resolve_store_target() -> Path:
2002:         return _resolve_target(file_arg, vertex_path)
2003: 
2004:     def fetch():
2005:         path = _resolve_store_target().resolve()
2006:         if not path.exists():
2007:             raise FileNotFoundError(f"{path} does not exist")
2008:         data = make_fetcher(path, zoom=3, kind=known.kind)()
2009:         # Lead the MINIMAL one-liner with the store name (spine dot-grammar),
2010:         # matching the store ticks/stats surfaces — the vertex name is the
2011:         # store stem, known here at the call site.
2012:         data.setdefault("vertex", path.stem)
2013:         return data
2014: 
2015:     def renderer(data, fidelity, width):
2016:         from ..lenses.store import store_view
2017:         from loops.lens_resolver import zoom_from_fidelity
2018:         return store_view(data, zoom_from_fidelity(fidelity), width)
2019: 
2020:     async def fetch_stream():
2021:         import asyncio
2022: 
2023:         while True:
2024:             try:
2025:                 yield fetch()
2026:             except FileNotFoundError:
2027:                 pass
2028:             await asyncio.sleep(2.0)
2029: 
2030:     def handle_interactive(ctx):
2031:         import asyncio as _asyncio
2032:         from ..tui import StoreExplorerApp
2033: 
2034:         path = _resolve_store_target().resolve()
2035:         app = StoreExplorerApp(path)
2036:         _asyncio.run(app.run())
2037:         return 0
2038: 
2039:     return run_cli(
2040:         rest,
2041:         fetch=fetch,
2042:         fetch_stream=fetch_stream,
2043:         renderer=renderer,
2044:         handlers={OutputMode.INTERACTIVE: handle_interactive},
2045:         default_mode=OutputMode.STATIC,
2046:         prog="loops store",
2047:         description=(
2048:             "Inspect store contents. Subcommands: "
2049:             "'loops store verify [target]' checks the tick hash chain; "
2050:             "'loops store rebirth <source> <target>' replays a store "
2051:             "through a transform with a verifiable receipt; "
2052:             "'loops store migrate <source> --vertex <target>' migrates a "
2053:             "legacy store into a fresh arrival lineage with a signed "
2054:             "migration report; "
2055:             "'loops store reanchor <vertex>' recomputes chain hashes; "
2056:             "'loops store absorb <vertex>' opens the declaration lineage "
2057:             "with a signed genesis event; "
2058:             "'loops store reindex <vertex>' drops and rebuilds the FTS "
2059:             "search index (the sole writer — reads stay read-only)."
2060:         ),
2061:         help_args=help_args,
2062:     )
2063: 
2064: 
2065: def make_fidelity_fetcher(path: Path):
2066:     """Create a fetcher for fidelity drill data.
2067: 
2068:     Returns a callable (since_ts, until_ts, kind?) -> list[dict].
2069:     Each call opens and closes its own StoreReader — the TUI calls this
2070:     on-demand when the user presses 'f', not on every frame.
2071:     """
2072:     def fetch(since_ts: float, until_ts: float, kind: str | None = None) -> list[dict]:
2073:         from engine.store_reader import StoreReader
2074: 
2075:         store_path = resolve_store_path(path)
2076:         with StoreReader(store_path) as reader:
2077:             return reader.facts_between(since_ts, until_ts, kind=kind)
2078:     return fetch

=== apps/loops/tests/test_store_absorb.py ===
1: """S1 — genesis + absorb ceremony (SPEC §9.2 era opening).
2: 
3: Exercises the REAL composition: parse a .vertex → signed ``_decl.genesis``
4: event (whole declaration document + protocol version + era pins), the
5: lineage-identity / no-force / must-be-signed rules, and the write-time
6: reservation of the ``_decl.*`` namespace on the emit path.
7: """
8: 
9: from __future__ import annotations
10: 
11: import argparse
12: import json
13: import sqlite3
14: from pathlib import Path
15: 
16: import pytest
17: 
18: from engine.builder import fold_count, vertex
19: from lang.document import DECL_GENESIS
20: from loops.commands.store import _run_absorb
21: from sign import ed25519
22: 
23: 
24: def _make_vertex(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:
25:     vpath = tmp_path / "x.vertex"
26:     (vertex("x").store("./x.db")
27:         .loop("ping", fold_count("n"), boundary_every=1)
28:         .write(vpath))
29:     return vpath
30: 
31: 
32: def _declare_key(vpath: Path, name: str, key_b64: str) -> None:
33:     vpath.write_text(
34:         vpath.read_text()
35:         + f'\nobservers {{\n  {name} {{\n    key "{key_b64}"\n  }}\n}}\n'
36:     )
37: 
38: 
39: def _make_signed_vertex(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:
40:     """Vertex + co-located keypair + self-observer key declaration."""
41:     vpath = _make_vertex(tmp_path)
42:     kp = ed25519.load_or_generate(tmp_path / "keys")
43:     _declare_key(vpath, "x", kp.public_b64)
44:     return vpath
45: 
46: 
47: def _emit(vertex_path: Path, kind: str, **payload):
48:     from loops.main import cmd_emit
49:     parts = [f"{k}={v}" for k, v in payload.items()]
50:     ns = argparse.Namespace(vertex=None, kind=kind, parts=parts, observer="", dry_run=False)
51:     return cmd_emit(ns, vertex_path=vertex_path)
52: 
53: 
54: def _genesis_row(db_path: Path) -> tuple | None:
55:     conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))
56:     try:
57:         return conn.execute(
58:             "SELECT id, kind, observer, payload, signature FROM facts WHERE kind = ?",
59:             (DECL_GENESIS,),
60:         ).fetchone()
61:     finally:
62:         conn.close()

115: class TestAbsorbPins:
116:     def test_pins_reflect_existing_facts_and_ticks(self, tmp_path):
117:         vpath = _make_signed_vertex(tmp_path)
118:         # boundary_every=1 → each emit also seals a signed tick.
119:         _emit(vpath, "ping", service="api")
120:         _emit(vpath, "ping", service="web")
121: 
122:         db = tmp_path / "x.db"
123:         conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db))
124:         newest_fact = conn.execute(
125:             "SELECT id FROM facts ORDER BY rowid DESC LIMIT 1"
126:         ).fetchone()[0]
127:         conn.close()
128: 
129:         # The actual latest-chained-tick row hash — what a successor's
130:         # prev_hash would commit to. absorb writes no tick, so this is
131:         # unchanged by the absorb and must equal the pinned chain_head.
132:         from atoms import Fact
133:         from engine.sqlite_store import SqliteStore
134:         s = SqliteStore(
135:             path=db, serialize=lambda f: f.to_dict(), deserialize=Fact.from_dict
136:         )
137:         expected_head = s.current_chain_head()
138:         s.close()
139:         assert expected_head is not None
140: 
141:         rc = _run_absorb(["--observer", "x"], vertex_path=vpath)
142:         assert rc == 0
143: 
144:         payload = json.loads(_genesis_row(db)[3])
145:         # fact_cursor pins the newest PRE-genesis fact (witness order).
146:         assert payload["fact_cursor"] == newest_fact
147:         # chain_head EQUALS the store's actual latest-tick chain hash.
148:         assert payload["chain_head"] == expected_head
149:         assert len(payload["chain_head"]) == 64  # sha256 hexdigest
150: 
151:     def test_read_absorption_state_agrees_with_chain_head_under_permutation(
152:         self, tmp_path: Path
153:     ) -> None:
154:         """On a permuted store with ticks whose rowid order and arrival order disagree,
155:         _read_absorption_state's chain head equals SqliteStore.current_chain_head().
156:         """
157:         from atoms import Fact
158:         from engine.sqlite_store import (
159:             FACT_INSERT_SQL,
160:             TICK_INSERT_SQL,
161:             SqliteStore,
162:             _tick_row_hash,
163:         )
164:         from loops.commands.store import _read_absorption_state
165: 
166:         db_path = tmp_path / "permuted_absorb.db"
167:         conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path), autocommit=True)
168:         store = SqliteStore(
169:             path=db_path,
170:             serialize=lambda f: f.to_dict(),
171:             deserialize=Fact.from_dict,
172:         )
173: 
174:         facts = [
175:             ("f-0", "note", 100.0, "kyle", "", '{"v": 0}', None, 0, 0),
176:             ("f-1", "note", 101.0, "kyle", "", '{"v": 1}', None, 1, 0),
177:             ("f-2", "note", 102.0, "kyle", "", '{"v": 2}', None, 2, 0),
178:         ]
179:         for row in facts:
180:             conn.execute(FACT_INSERT_SQL, row)
181: 
182:         # Precompute tick hashes on arrival coordinate axis
183:         t1_hash = store._window_hash("", "f-1")
184:         t1_row = (
185:             "t-1", "seal", 101.5, None, "test", '{"n": 1}', None, "", "f-1", t1_hash, None
186:         )
187:         t1_row_hash = _tick_row_hash(t1_row)
188: 
189:         t2_hash = store._window_hash("f-1", "f-2")
190:         t2_row = (
191:             "t-2", "seal", 102.5, None, "test", '{"n": 2}', t1_row_hash, "f-1", "f-2", t2_hash, None
192:         )
193:         t2_row_hash = _tick_row_hash(t2_row)
194: 
195:         # Insert tick 2 (coord 5,0) before tick 1 (coord 4,0) -> t-2 gets rowid 1, t-1 gets rowid 2
196:         conn.execute(
197:             TICK_INSERT_SQL,
198:             ("t-2", "seal", 102.5, None, "test", '{"n": 2}', t1_row_hash, "f-1", "f-2", t2_hash, None, 5, 0),
199:         )
200:         conn.execute(
201:             TICK_INSERT_SQL,
202:             ("t-1", "seal", 101.5, None, "test", '{"n": 1}', None, "", "f-1", t1_hash, None, 4, 0),
203:         )
204: 
205:         # Disagreement check: rowid order has t-1 as newest; arrival order has t-2 as newest
206:         tick_rowids = [r[0] for r in conn.execute("SELECT id FROM ticks ORDER BY rowid").fetchall()]
207:         assert tick_rowids == ["t-2", "t-1"]
208: 
209:         tick_arrivals = [
210:             r[0] for r in conn.execute("SELECT id FROM ticks ORDER BY arrival_ordinal, arrival_seq").fetchall()
211:         ]
212:         assert tick_arrivals == ["t-1", "t-2"]
213: 
214:         expected_chain_head = store.current_chain_head()
215:         assert expected_chain_head == t2_row_hash
216:         assert expected_chain_head != t1_row_hash
217: 
218:         has_genesis, chain_head, fact_cursor, has_marker = _read_absorption_state(db_path)
219:         assert chain_head is not None
220:         assert chain_head == expected_chain_head
221:         assert chain_head == t2_row_hash
222:         assert chain_head != t1_row_hash
223: 
224:         conn.close()
225:         store.close()
226:

308: # ===========================================================================
309: # S4 — edit ceremony (re-absorb diffs at subject granularity)
310: # ===========================================================================
311: 
312: 
313: def _signed_vertex_text(key_b64: str, loops: list[str]) -> str:
314:     body = 'name "x"\nstore "./x.db"\nloops {\n'
315:     for name in loops:
316:         body += f'  {name} {{ fold {{ n "inc" }} }}\n'
317:     body += "}\n"
318:     body += f'observers {{\n  x {{\n    key "{key_b64}"\n  }}\n}}\n'
319:     return body
320: 
321: 
322: def _make_editable_vertex(tmp_path: Path, loops: list[str]) -> tuple[Path, str]:
323:     """A signed vertex whose text we can rewrite to simulate a file edit."""
324:     vpath = tmp_path / "x.vertex"
325:     kp = ed25519.load_or_generate(tmp_path / "keys")
326:     vpath.write_text(_signed_vertex_text(kp.public_b64, loops))
327:     return vpath, kp.public_b64
328: 
329: 
330: def _decl_rows(db_path: Path, kind: str) -> list[dict]:
331:     conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))
332:     try:
333:         rows = conn.execute(
334:             "SELECT payload FROM facts WHERE kind = ? ORDER BY rowid", (kind,)
335:         ).fetchall()
336:     finally:
337:         conn.close()
338:     return [json.loads(r[0]) for r in rows]
339:

341: class TestEditModeReconcile:
342:     def test_edit_adds_kind_and_resolver_reflects_it(self, tmp_path):
343:         from engine.declaration import load_declaration

346:         assert _run_absorb(["--observer", "x"], vertex_path=vpath) == 0

351:

353:         assert _run_absorb(["--observer", "x"], vertex_path=vpath) == 0
354:         defined = _decl_rows(tmp_path / "x.db", "_decl.kind-defined")
355:         assert any(d["subject"] == "c" and d["change"] == "added" for d in defined)

485:     def test_reabsorb_leaves_default_visible_total_unchanged(self, tmp_path):
486:         """S4 regression: re-absorbing an edit grows the `_decl.*` row count, but
487:         the DEFAULT visible fact total (SPEC §9.4 exclusion) must not move — only
488:         the include_internal total grows. Guards store/stats and ls share math."""
489:         from engine.store_reader import StoreReader
490: 
491:         vpath, key = _make_editable_vertex(tmp_path, ["a"])
492:         # Emit a couple of ordinary facts so the visible total is non-zero.
493:         _emit(vpath, "ping", service="api")
494:         _emit(vpath, "ping", service="web")
495:         assert _run_absorb(["--observer", "x"], vertex_path=vpath) == 0
496: 
497:         db = tmp_path / "x.db"
498:         with StoreReader(db) as reader:
499:             visible_before = reader.fact_total
500:             internal_before = reader.fact_total_all()
501:         assert visible_before == 2  # the two ping facts; genesis excluded
502: 
503:         # Edit + re-absorb → appends _decl.kind-defined rows.
504:         vpath.write_text(_signed_vertex_text(key, ["a", "b", "c"]))
505:         assert _run_absorb(["--observer", "x"], vertex_path=vpath) == 0
506: 
507:         with StoreReader(db) as reader:
508:             visible_after = reader.fact_total
509:             internal_after = reader.fact_total_all()
510:         assert visible_after == visible_before  # default total UNCHANGED by the edit
511:         assert internal_after > internal_before  # the escape hatch sees the growth

=== docs/libs/sdk/ADOPTION.md ===
1: # Adopt a migrated Arrival declaration
2: 
3: `sdk.adopt_arrival` appends the declaration anchor that makes a migrated
4: Arrival history usable by ordinary SDK reads and writes. It preserves the
5: migration prefix and uses the existing physical lineage. Fresh stores use
6: `init_vertex`; stores with an adopted declaration use `edit_declaration`.
7: 
8: The caller supplies two distinct snapshots:
9: 
10: - `reviewed_text` and its `reviewed_sha256`: exact declaration bytes selected
11:   for adoption, such as the copied legacy vertex used for migration.
12: - `declaration_text`: the current published vertex, naming an explicit backend,
13:   physical lineage, and authority role.
14: 
15: Their declaration documents must agree apart from residence. This first
16: operation cannot introduce or replace observer keys, or adopt a changed
17: declaration as an incidental part of migration. It checks the selected head,
18: registry-forming signatures, declaration identities and captured observer keys
19: before signing the new anchor.
20: It independently verifies the resulting FACT and ARRIVAL signatures against
21: the selected mapped binding's public key; a provider-local verifier cannot
22: replace that check.
23: It also checks that the selected backend provides projection catch-up before
24: resolving signing credentials; the preflight does not advance the projection.
25: 
26: ```python
27: from sdk import adopt_arrival
28: 
29: # Keep these snapshots and the report verification receipt from the offline
30: # migration workflow. Verify its report at selected_head BEFORE adoption.
31: result = adopt_arrival(
32:     published_vertex,
33:     selected_head=migration.head,
34:     reviewed_text=reviewed_text,
35:     reviewed_sha256=reviewed_sha256,
36:     declaration_text=published_vertex.read_text(encoding="utf-8"),
37:     observer="alice",
38:     credentials=mapped_provider,
39: )
40: receipt = result.as_dict()
41: ```
42: 
43: The mapped binding must already exist and match the observer's captured key
44: history. Adoption resolves distinct FACT and ARRIVAL authorship requests;
45: neither lookup creates keys nor falls back to legacy credentials. The result
46: retains the adoption commit, projection outcome and public binding evidence.
47: Migration report verification remains the offline caller's responsibility;
48: the report's live-head comparison no longer applies after adoption advances
49: the history.
50: 
51: Before appending, the operation durably reserves the exact signed draft in
52: `<vertex>.arrival-adopt.intent`. If interrupted, retain the intent and the
53: typed error's commit/head evidence, then reconcile explicitly:
54: 
55: ```python
56: from sdk import recover_arrival_adoption
57: 
58: recovered = recover_arrival_adoption(intent_path)
59: ```
60: 
61: A stale apply may leave this intent reserved; use its `intent_path` for explicit
62: recovery before attempting a fresh adoption. Maintenance capability is checked
63: again before apply or recovery appends, using the registry supplied to that call.
64: 
65: Recovery requires no signing credentials. It either appends the reserved draft
66: at its original predecessor or recognizes that exact draft at its expected
67: ordinal, synchronizes the projection and checks the published cache. It does
68: not re-sign or append a duplicate. If later records exist, `head` still names
69: the adoption head; `observed_head` records the later tip. A reconstructed commit
70: labels its durability evidence as recovery evidence rather than claiming the
71: original append receipt was retained.
72: 
73: If recovery Full-verifies that another record has taken the reserved next
74: ordinal, it retires only that provably superseded intent and returns a stale
75: refusal. A caller can then prepare a fresh adoption at the new selected head.
76: Malformed intents also return a typed SDK refusal and remain in place for
77: operator inspection. Reviewed source, template and parameter pins must remain
78: intact during recovery, as they must for normal runtime declaration resolution.
79: 
80: The operation reconciles the already-published cache without rewriting it.
81: A changed cache or a post-append projection failure leaves a recovery boundary;
82: a durable append is not rolled back. Process-exit tests cover reservation and
83: append boundaries, not arbitrary power loss. Live writer coordination and a
84: representative user-store rehearsal remain separate from this SDK operation.
85: 
86: Adoption alone does not prove continuity for older boundary ticks. If preserved
87: legacy ticks would be consumed as current loop resets or vertex periods but
88: predate the adopted declaration, ordinary writes can refuse with
89: `BoundaryContinuityRefused`. Migration, adoption, reads and export can succeed
90: while writes remain unavailable. The loops real-store rehearsal demonstrated
91: this case; do not bypass the continuity check or silently discard those ticks.
92: To explicitly begin fresh execution state, select the signed adoption policy:
93: 
94: ```python
95: result = adopt_arrival(
96:     target,
97:     selected_head=migration.head,
98:     reviewed_text=reviewed_text,
99:     reviewed_sha256=reviewed_sha256,
100:     declaration_text=published_text,
101:     observer="project",
102:     credentials=mapped_provider,
103:     runtime_epoch="fresh",
104: )
105: ```
106: 
107: The default `runtime_epoch="strict"` preserves the existing continuity checks.
108: Fresh mode records declaration protocol 2 and the exact signed marker
109: `fresh-after-anchor-v1`; older protocol-1 readers refuse it. It starts execution
110: folds at their declared initial values, counters at zero, and period context
111: without any old tick. Only facts and ticks received after the adoption anchor
112: participate in runtime replay and source cadence, regardless of their event
113: timestamps. Post-adoption continuity checks remain in force.
114: 
115: `read_state` reflects this execution epoch and includes mode/anchor evidence in
116: its generation metadata; aggregate state applies each member's own floor.
117: `read_summary`, fact/tick queries and export retain all historical rows. Global
118: duplicate-ID checks and physical tick predecessor/window hashes also retain
119: all history: starting fresh execution does not reset the custody chain.
120: 
121: Recovery reads the mode from the intent field, checks it against the exact
122: reserved draft payload, and independently verifies the draft's FACT and Arrival
123: signatures. It exposes the recovered `runtime_epoch` and takes no policy
124: override. An existing adopted
125: store cannot be reset by editing its locator or passing a new recovery option.
126: Select fresh mode when preparing the explicit adoption of an unadopted copy.

=== docs/scratch/arrival-break/adoption-fresh-runtime-epoch-design-2026-09-18.md ===
1: # Explicit fresh runtime epoch at adoption
2: 
3: ## Decision and scope
4: 
5: The real loops-store rehearsal (`2a75980c`) preserved 121 old ticks, including
6: owned vertex-period ticks before the new declaration anchor. C6 correctly
7: refuses consuming those ticks without proven declaration continuity. The user
8: selected “Start a new boundary epoch at adoption; define its initial state.”
9: This design adds an explicit opt-in execution cutover. It does not invent
10: historical declarations or reinterpret old ticks as proven boundary events.
11: 
12: ## Initial state
13: 
14: Let S be the reviewed migrated head and A the own declaration anchor appended
15: at S+1. For a fresh epoch:
16: 
17: - Each runtime fold starts at its declared identity/initial value. No old fact
18:   seeds its mutable execution state. Static declarations and pinned source
19:   configuration still apply.
20: - Boundary counters start at zero; one-shot boundaries are not exhausted;
21:   there are no pending old boundaries, old loop resets, or old vertex-period
22:   timestamps. The normal first post-A event starts period context.
23: - Runtime replay consumes only facts/ticks whose physical Arrival ordinal is
24:   strictly greater than A. A backdated post-A event remains in this epoch;
25:   wall-clock/event timestamps never select the execution floor.
26: - Post-A declarations and ticks retain all existing C6 continuity/refusal
27:   checks. A fresh marker is not permission to ignore later corruption, name
28:   reuse, unknown generated roles, or declaration discontinuities.
29: 
30: ## Execution state versus custody evidence
31: 
32: Filtering applies to runtime hydration, count/reset/period interpretation,
33: source cadence and pending work, and the public current `read_state` view.
34: Aggregate current state applies each member's own floor independently.
35: 
36: Filtering does **not** apply to source preservation, raw fact/tick queries,
37: summary counts, Full verification, exact export, or global ID/idempotency checks.
38: An old ID cannot be reused merely because it predates the epoch.
39: 
40: The physical tick chain also remains global: `prev_hash`, fact cursors, and
41: window hashes continue the custody chain across A, using all required historical
42: rows. An old tick can remain the cryptographic predecessor without becoming
43: a runtime reset/count/period edge. Resetting that chain would be a different
44: wire/audit change and is excluded.
45: 
46: ## Signed representation and compatibility
47: 
48: SDK adoption receives `runtime_epoch="strict" | "fresh"`, default `strict`.
49: Strict emits the current protocol-1 genesis without an epoch marker and keeps
50: the current behavior. Initialization continues to emit protocol 1.
51: 
52: Fresh emits declaration protocol 2 with the exact payload marker
53: `"runtime_epoch": "fresh-after-anchor-v1"` alongside the reviewed documents.
54: The existing FACT and ARRIVAL signatures cover this payload. Protocol 2 is
55: required so protocol-1 readers refuse instead of silently replaying old state.
56: The physical Arrival wire grammar is unchanged.
57: 
58: Central payload validation accepts the supported combinations only: v1 has no
59: epoch marker; v2 requires the exact marker. Unsupported/malformed combinations
60: refuse. No declaration edit can invent or move this immutable genesis floor.
61: The own store-local anchor must match physical lineage, fact identity/kind and
62: its actual receipt ordinal; foreign merged genesis facts cannot select a floor.
63: No mutable locator flag or free-standing projection metadata grants this mode.
64: At runtime the projected anchor is compared with the exact physical anchor from
65: the attested custody read, including its payload and signatures; both inventing
66: and stripping the marker in the projection refuse. This is not a new claim to
67: independently reverify every historical signature on every read. The supported
68: SDK prepare/recovery paths independently verify both domains before publishing
69: the anchor; deliberately false low-level injected verifiers/raw ledger writes
70: remain the existing trusted low-level API boundary.
71: 
72: Recovery takes no caller-supplied epoch override. It reads the reserved mode
73: from the intent field, cross-checks it against the exact draft payload,
74: reconstructs the same canonical payload, and independently verifies both
75: signature domains. Marker tampering cannot
76: change the epoch while retaining authentic signatures. Results expose the mode
77: and anchor; `head` and Commit(S,A) retain their existing meanings.
78: 
79: ## Rehearsal and acceptance
80: 
81: Keep the strict rehearsal and its refusal evidence unchanged. Run a fresh
82: rehearsal on a separate copied descriptor and output, using the same audited
83: prepared source. Preserve the original and prepared source hashes.
84: 
85: Required coverage:
86: 
87: 1. Default strict mode still refuses consumed pre-genesis owned ticks.
88: 2. Fresh mode starts empty/declared state at A while historical query counts
89:    and exact export retain all old rows and signatures.
90: 3. Ordinary signed writes and a new boundary tick succeed; current state sees
91:    post-A facts only, including backdated arrivals.
92: 4. Tick-chain audit remains valid across the execution epoch; global duplicate
93:    detection still sees pre-A IDs.
94: 5. Post-A reset/count/period behavior, declaration continuity, source cadence,
95:    and aggregate state follow the same floor without weakening later checks.
96: 6. Malformed/foreign epoch evidence refuses; protocol-1 readers reject the
97:    fresh payload; default initialization and old anchors are unchanged.
98: 7. Both interruption boundaries recover the same signed epoch and exact anchor
99:    without credentials, including refusal of self-consistent tampered markers.
100: 8. Rerun the real loops copy through reads, writes, exact export and recovery.
101: 
102: Sol owns engine/lang implementation; Terra owns SDK/state/rehearsal integration;
103: Luna provides adversarial review; root owns integration acceptance and evidence.

=== docs/scratch/arrival-break/consistency-c6-boundary-design-2026-09-06.md ===
1: # C6 — boundary continuity design
2: 
3: Status: accepted by root after two Fable 5.1 **high** design reviews and
4: source/test-backed triage. Implementation is now authorized within this design.
5: The follow-up raw verdict remains REVISE; its sole new blocker was disproved
6: by the existing params-pin gate. See the retained review triage.
7: Baseline `ef8b21b2` on `arrival/finish` includes
8: the accepted C3 continuation checkpoint. This document is the proposed decision,
9: not a claim that current runtime already enforces it. Sol audits engine history
10: and replay, Terra SDK operations, Luna independent failure cases, and root owns
11: the policy and review triage.
12: 
13: ## Decision and scope
14: 
15: A continuing boundary keeps its recorded consumption history. An ordinary
16: declaration edit changes the current interpretation of facts; it does not
17: restart that boundary. The proposed identity for this supported subset remains
18: the exact local vertex name, boundary role, and boundary name. No declaration
19: hash, file path, projection generation, or new wire identifier becomes identity.
20: 
21: The recommended policy is **inherit for in-place edits**. A trigger/count,
22: condition, fold, parse, route, or dispatch edit keeps the existing tick edge.
23: This is an explicit interpretation policy, not a claim that the old tick would
24: have fired under the new rules. A pending fact already consumed by that edge
25: does not become eligible again just because a declaration changed.
26: 
27: Whole-loop retirement followed by same-name recreation needs separate evidence
28: when an old local tick exists. A tick whose loop/vertex role was ambiguous when
29: received also cannot become unambiguous through a later configuration edit.
30: Those cases refuse runtime when the target consumes that ambiguous stream.
31: Removing the consumer is allowed; reintroducing it re-exposes the same conflict.
32: Facts, ticks, and declarations
33: remain available as evidence; no prior row is changed or relabeled.
34: 
35: The user has been offered a choice between inherited in-place trigger/count
36: edits and a stricter interim refusal of those edits. No answer has arrived as
37: this draft is prepared for review; root proceeds with the recommended inherit
38: policy as a stated assumption. Any later answer and the adversarial design
39: review must be reconciled before implementation. Credential requests and persisted bindings (D0/D2), explicit
40: same-name restart/carry ceremonies, and durable vertex IDs are separate work.
41: 
42: ## What the implementation actually does today
43: 
44: `Vertex.hydrate_snapshot` re-folds captured facts under the current declaration
45: in receipt order. It does **not** deserialize an old tick payload as current
46: fold state. A local loop tick invokes `Loop.replay_boundary`: reset to the
47: current initial state when reset is enabled, clear repeating-count bookkeeping,
48: or mark a one-shot boundary exhausted. A local vertex tick restores the vertex
49: period timestamp and does not reset every loop.
50: 
51: `plan_pending_boundaries` selects the greatest event-time/receipt edge per local
52: tick name. Facts at an equal timestamp precede or follow that edge by receipt
53: coordinate. This remains distinct from the tick's fact cursor and from
54: declaration receipt ordering. Count boundaries remain ingress-driven.
55: 
56: The grammar currently exposes `when`, `after`, and `every`; its compiler always
57: sets loop-boundary `reset=True`. The lower-level `Loop` supports carry, but
58: there is no public declaration reset/carry edit to implement in C6. Fold
59: overrides replace initial state and fold code, not declared boundary reset.
60: 
61: Consequently an in-place count edit retains the recorded boundary and replays
62: facts after it. The exact existing count-recovery behavior is:
63: 
64: | Target mode and retained evidence | Hydrated state and next behavior |
65: | --- | --- |
66: | `every M`, with an owned tick and K subsequently routed facts | Count is K, including when K >= M. Hydration does not fire. If K >= M, the next accepted routed ingress fires; otherwise M-K more routed facts are needed. |
67: | `every M`, with no owned tick and K replayed facts | Existing recovery sets count to K modulo M. It creates no missing historical ticks. |
68: | `after M`, with any owned loop tick | Inherits an exhausted one-shot boundary. New ingress does not restart it. |
69: | `after M`, with no owned tick and K replayed facts | Exhausted when K >= M; otherwise count is K. A fresh name can therefore already be exhausted by older facts. |
70: | `after` changed in place to `every M`, with an old owned tick | Current repeating mode resumes from that retained tick and subsequent routed facts, not from the declaration edit. |
71: | Kind-trigger edit with an owned tick | Pending evaluation retains the old event-time/receipt edge; only unconsumed facts are checked against the new trigger/conditions. |
72: 
73: A user wanting a fresh count or first firing is requesting a restart; C6 does
74: not silently provide one. Historical payloads keep their original meaning and
75: bytes even when current replay differs. Count-return values ignored during
76: hydration must not turn into retrospective dispatch or appended ticks.
77: 
78: Sources: `libs/engine/src/engine/vertex.py:784`, `:1260`;
79: `libs/engine/src/engine/loop.py:157`;
80: `libs/engine/src/engine/compiler.py:541`, `:890`;
81: `libs/lang/src/lang/document.py:463`, `:1100`.
82: 
83: ## Contract matrix
84: 
85: | Situation at the bounded head | Supported result |
86: | --- | --- |
87: | Same continuously present loop, unchanged declaration | Inherit its recorded reset/count/period and pending consumption evidence. |
88: | Same continuously present loop, edited fold/parse/routes/trigger/count/conditions/run or presentation | Inherit the same edge and replay under the new interpretation; no implicit restart. |
89: | No local ticks for a loop; edit, remove/recreate, or introduce a boundary | Allowed, subject to existing checks. Older facts may replay and pending triggers may become eligible; this is not a fresh-facts cutoff. |
90: | Remove a loop that has old ticks | Allowed when it is absent from the proposed runtime. Preserve old ticks as evidence. |
91: | Reintroduce that retired runtime name after it had a local tick | Refuse ordinary declaration preparation/preview and runtime capture. A final-document comparison alone cannot detect this. |
92: | Add a genuinely unused loop name | Allowed. It has a distinct future tick identity, but may still consume older facts selected by current routing. This is not state carry or an explicit restart. |
93: | Vertex boundary list edited in place | Inherit the vertex-name edge. Declaration order remains meaningful; no new vertex identity. |
94: | Historical tick had `origin == name == vertex` while that loop also existed | Refuse when the target has a vertex boundary. A corrected target without that consumer may run, but adding a vertex boundary later refuses. The old row remains ambiguous. |
95: | Historical collision existed but produced no ambiguous local tick | Permit a collision-free append-forward correction. Preserve C5's existing accepted case. |
96: | Foreign or originless tick | Keep as evidence; it does not establish a local edge or a local incarnation conflict. Equal labels in another lineage prove no identity. |
97: | Owned tick predates the own declaration genesis | Refuse if the target consumes its loop name or vertex period: no recorded declaration establishes its role. Removing that consumer can avoid the conflict; a new name is not a historical alias. |
98: | Tick could belong to a generated name whose historical expansion is not recorded | Refuse the affected runtime interpretation as unprovable; current files/environment cannot provide historical evidence. |
99: | Explicit restart, state carry, alias, or same-name incarnation cutover | Deferred new protocol behavior; not inferred from an ordinary edit, projection rebuild, or locator move. |
100: 
101: Removing an explicit `cite` declaration still leaves the implicit runtime
102: `cite` loop. That is an in-place interpretation change, not retirement of the
103: runtime name. Likewise, disabling a loop's boundary leaves the loop present;
104: old ticks still reset it under current hydration. The declaration representation
105: and the effective runtime namespace must not be conflated.
106: 
107: ## History and evidence algorithm
108: 
109: The shared validator is pure over one captured declaration anchor, complete
110: self-lineage declaration fact history, bounded ticks, and the target document
111: set (effective current state for runtime, proposed successor for editing).
112: It does not open a store, sign, read keys, consult external source files or the
113: environment, repair projections, or execute any source/dispatch command.
114: 
115: 1. Validate the declaration anchor against the captured full H through the
116:    existing custody/projection contract. Select only the own genesis and
117:    self-lineage declaration overlays. Foreign and unscoped declarations remain
118:    inert, matching the existing resolver. Sort by receipt ordinal and sequence,
119:    never authored time or fact ID.
120: 2. Reconstruct effective runtime names at the genesis and each committed
121:    declaration revision. Preserve retirement evidence across intermediate
122:    revisions; do not compare only genesis/current or before/proposed documents.
123:    Multiple declaration documents in one Arrival batch form one revision:
124:    evaluate the resulting effective set, not transient partial document states
125:    with no observable runtime between them. This grouping is for namespace
126:    continuity; document overlay order remains receipt-sequence order. Wire
127:    batches contain fact rows only; a CAS containing fact and tick drafts gives
128:    those drafts distinct record ordinals. A query reporting an owned tick and
129:    declaration fact at the same ordinal contradicts this shape and refuses
130:    with both actual coordinates. Do not invent an ordering for that adapter
131:    contradiction. This is not a newly legal mixed-row record format.
132: 3. At each owned tick's receipt position, classify its role using that revision.
133:    A vertex-name tick with a simultaneously present loop of that name is
134:    ambiguous. A currently relevant loop-name tick with no provable historical
135:    loop membership is unproven. Tick payload shape, signature, cursor, or
136:    current name validity cannot recover the missing role evidence.
137: 4. For each target runtime name with historical local ticks, require continuous
138:    membership since the relevant tick. A disappearance and later return
139:    conflicts even if its final declaration equals the earlier one. Internal
140:    tracking of membership intervals is evidence analysis, not a newly minted
141:    persistent incarnation ID. Ignore retired names that the target does not
142:    interpret. An ambiguous vertex-name tick matters when the target declares
143:    a vertex boundary; without that consumer the saved period field has no
144:    supported Arrival effect. Later re-enabling that boundary restores the
145:    refusal, not the missing role evidence.
146: 5. Analyze the proposed successor as one additional revision with no fabricated
147:    fact ID, timestamp, ordinal, or Commit. This allows retirement and safe
148:    configuration correction even when the old runtime itself refuses. Run
149:    the check for no-op proposals too; no-op does not certify unsafe execution.
150: 6. Return normally or a deterministic diagnostic naming the reason, boundary,
151:    and actual conflicting tick/declaration receipt where available. Do not
152:    manufacture an epoch/cutoff or report guessed identity as proven.
153: 
154: The custody-bounded query is still an adapter contract: completing a watermark
155: does not independently establish row agreement or prove a projected declaration
156: was authored by the claimed observer. C6 adds continuity analysis, not a second
157: custody audit or new tick-authentication rule.
158: 
159: The evidence read is through captured H even when event timestamps are negative
160: or far in the future. `TickRequest` defaults to `since=0`; the validator must
161: explicitly request the full timestamp range. Existing hydration/pending
162: timestamp selection is a separate policy and must not be widened accidentally.
163: 
164: The later fresh-epoch adoption implementation made that split explicit. Strict
165: runtime hydration and pending-boundary execution still select ticks with
166: `ts >= 0`, preserving the previous `TickRequest()` reset behavior. Continuity
167: evidence and physical tick predecessor/window calculations select the full
168: receipt prefix, including negative event times. An explicitly fresh runtime
169: epoch selects post-anchor ticks by receipt ordinal, including negative times.
170: 
171: Use documents as the common target input for all three entrypoints. The result
172: includes the target's provable runtime names and unresolved-generator flag.
173: Apply C5 reservation to provable names, including literal generated loops,
174: during declaration preview and preparation as well as runtime. Runtime also
175: checks names in both directions: every provable target name must be materialized
176: (including implicit `cite`), and every actual compiled name must belong to the
177: proved set or be covered by an explicitly unknown generator. An unexplained
178: extra or missing name refuses before hydration. Thus current parameter-file
179: changes between verification and compilation cannot silently remove a proved
180: consumer. This checks names, not arbitrary fold-code equivalence.
181: 
182: Unchanged wire declaration decoding remains authoritative. Prefer sharing the
183: existing overlay step/ordering code rather than introducing a second subtly
184: different definition of self-lineage history. Unknown declaration kinds retain
185: the resolver's forward-compatible inert treatment; malformed supported
186: namespace evidence refuses rather than yielding a fabricated safe history.
187: 
188: ## Generated loop names
189: 
190: Only template sources with a declared `loop` generate runtime loop specs in
191: the current compiler. Bare paths, inline source commands, and templates without
192: such a loop do not create additional loop identities. They must not trigger a
193: blanket source-history refusal.
194: 
195: For a template loop, literal inline parameter rows and the stored loop document
196: can establish generated names without opening the template file. Match the
197: compiler's actual ordering and override semantics: sources sorted by recorded
198: order/subject, parameter rows in order, generated specs overriding explicit
199: specs at equal names. Variables used only by collection commands do not make a
200: literal loop name unprovable.
201: 
202: A `from` parameter file's hash pin commits to historical bytes. If bytes
203: available now verify against the pin recorded for a revision, those bytes can
204: establish that revision's parameter rows. An absent, mismatched, or unpinned
205: file supplies no such evidence. Environment-indirected `kind` remains unknown
206: even in verified rows because the resolved environment value was not recorded.
207: Unknown generators can produce/override any name, including the vertex name.
208: 
209: Keep the classifier pure with explicit `verified_params: hash -> ordered rows`
210: evidence. A shared caller-side collector used by runtime, preparation, and
211: semantic preview obtains candidate bytes from captured historical parameter
212: paths relative to the locator, hashes those exact bytes, and accepts them only
213: for matching recorded pins. Parse the same byte snapshot that was hashed;
214: never verify a path and reopen it to parse potentially changed bytes. Reuse an
215: extracted form of the compiler's parameter-text parser, preserving encoding,
216: header, comments, row ordering, and last-column behavior. Do not implement a
217: second grammar. Cache by verified content hash within this capture only.
218: Unknown/unavailable evidence remains unknown and only blocks a relevant proof;
219: an irrelevant unreadable historical source must not poison a direct loop.
220: No network retrieval or environment resolution supplies historical evidence.
221: 
222: The classifier uses the same whole-value `$NAME` indirection recognition and
223: `$$` leading-dollar escape as the compiler. Partial strings remain literal,
224: matching current behavior. Parameter indirection used only by source commands
225: does not make a literal `kind` unknown. This evidence collector does not replace
226: current runtime pin verification and compilation, which retain their own checks.
227: 
228: That runtime pin gate already exists: `_build_effective_arrival_candidate`
229: calls `effective_declaration_from_documents` with default `verify_pins=True`;
230: the latter calls `verify_source_pins_from_documents`, which checks both source
231: content and `from.params_sha256` before `compile_sources`. Do not add a duplicate
232: gate based on the follow-up review's mistaken claim that it is absent.
233: Continuity here describes the recorded declaration and its pinned expansion,
234: not forensic proof that every past external program obeyed that declaration.
235: Unknown legacy behavior or an origin label is not upgraded into authentication.
236: 
237: Use three-valued membership per name and revision. Direct, implicit, or literal
238: generated membership proves **present** even if another generator has unknown
239: names: an override changes its interpretation but does not remove that name.
240: If the name is not in the provable set and an unresolved generator could emit
241: it, membership is **unknown**. Otherwise it is **absent**.
242: 
243: For every old local loop tick that the target would interpret, membership must
244: be provably present at its receipt and throughout all later revisions through
245: the target. Unknown membership anywhere in that interval refuses: an unknown
246: intervening revision could conceal retirement/recreation without containing a
247: tick itself. Analyze every relevant tick, not just the newest pending edge;
248: hydration also consumes historical reset markers. A tick whose name is absent
249: from the target is not attached to a current loop, so that retired stream alone
250: does not block execution. Unknown target membership for an old tick refuses.
251: 
252: For a vertex-name tick consumed by a target vertex boundary, loop membership
253: at its receipt must be provably absent.
254: Present means a role collision; unknown means its role cannot be established.
255: The vertex identity itself continues while its boundary list is edited or
256: disabled, so only loop-name ambiguity at that tick changes this role proof.
257: Unresolved revisions before all relevant ticks do not, by themselves, create
258: consumption obligations. This is a conservative evidence rule, not proof that
259: unknown expansions actually collided. Literal generated sources and provably
260: continuous direct loops must remain usable. Do not compile old declarations
261: against current external inputs or claim that no document edit proves stability.
262: 
263: ## Integration and effects
264: 
265: - Runtime: invoke the validator in the shared effective-candidate builder
266:   before `hydrate_snapshot`, pending-boundary planning, signer invocation, and source
267:   collection. Ordinary, batch, preview, and source paths inherit that one check.
268:   The lower-level caller-responsible planners remain internal; C6 does not
269:   pretend to validate an arbitrary caller-built `Vertex` against history.
270:   SDK credential providers may already have loaded signer capabilities before
271:   capture; C6 does not claim that loading never occurred or redesign providers.
272:   Raise `BoundaryContinuityRefused(RuntimeWriteRefused)` retaining the actual
273:   `basis`, `captured_head`, effective declaration, and a small immutable issue
274:   DTO. Expose actual `tick_id`, receipt `ordinal`, relevant declaration `fact_id`
275:   and `vertex` through existing C2 scalar evidence fields when known. The local
276:   planning attempt has phase `prepare` and custody append not entered; it does
277:   not erase prior successful source tiers or imply an external witness did not
278:   advance. The diagnostic contains no full source/record payloads.
279: - Declaration preparation: validate the proposed successor at the same CURRENT
280:   basis after history/custody checks and before key-history work that invokes
281:   signers or creates drafts/intents. Use `DeclarationPreparationRefused` with
282:   established prepare/custody-not-entered evidence and captured H. An accepted
283:   registry open may still advance an external witness; custody-not-entered
284:   refers to append, not an assertion of zero effects anywhere. Preserve the
285:   structured conflict as an explicit exception cause (`raise ... from ...`);
286:   C2 already serializes `__cause__`. A general cause parameter on every
287:   preparation refusal factory is unnecessary. Raise that explicit preparation
288:   refusal ahead of the generic basis-error wrapper, preserving a continuity
289:   diagnostic instead of mislabeling it as failure to capture the basis.
290: - SDK semantic preview: use the same bounded history/target validator, with its
291:   existing normalized refusal family and no invented signing/Commit evidence.
292:   A preview must not say applicable for a proposal that the history gate refuses.
293: - Reads/verify/export: do not add this execution gate to evidence reconstruction
294:   or ordinary read APIs. Read-state interpretation remains available and is not
295:   a certification of safe runtime continuity.
296: - Legacy attached `Vertex.replay`/`evaluate_boundaries` and `load_vertex_program`
297:   are outside this supported Arrival capture contract. Their separate origin
298:   and variable-substitution behavior remains legacy-retirement work; C6 must
299:   not claim to have made every historical CLI/runtime path conformant.
300: - Initialization has no old tick history. C5's pre-genesis name check remains;
301:   no pointless history pass or key migration is added.
302: - Existing declaration apply/recovery uses its frozen plan/durable intent.
303:   Runtime capture independently rejects any unsafe history imported or appended
304:   through other mechanisms. No recovery rewrite, automatic retry, or implicit
305:   transition is introduced.
306: 
307: ## Acceptance and staged work
308: 
309: Engine tests must reproduce the old acceptance of a ticked remove/recreate and
310: of a corrected historical loop/vertex collision. Assert the refusal happens
311: before hydration, signing, append, pending planning, or collection as appropriate.
312: Use full bounded receipt histories, including intermediate retirement, batched
313: changes, equal/out-of-order event times, foreign declarations/ticks, a relevant
314: undeclared historical tick, implicit `cite`, and stale local-file disagreement.
315: 
316: Positive engine and real SDK scenarios must demonstrate in-place fold and
317: boundary-count edits retaining edges, `after` inheriting exhaustion, tick-free
318: changes reinterpreting older facts, retirement followed by a fresh-name
319: replacement, unchanged/literal template sources, and tick-free C5 repair.
320: Keep evidence reads/export working for refused histories and verify exact
321: custody prefix preservation through an accepted appended correction.
322: 
323: Negative source scenarios cover missing/mismatched historical parameter pins
324: and env-derived generated names with old ticks even when source documents are
325: unchanged. Positive scenarios prove historical pin-matching rows work in all
326: three entrypoints, direct names stay provably present despite unrelated unknown
327: generators, and a file replacement between read/hash/parse cannot substitute
328: unverified rows. A command-only source edit with provable names remains usable.
329: Test ambiguous-history repair without a vertex consumer, followed by refusal
330: when one is proposed again. Exercise every row of the count-recovery table,
331: including a fresh-name `after` already exhausted by old facts.
332: 
333: Stages: root reconciles this design and agent audits; freeze exact design and
334: supporting source packet; Fable 5.1 high reviews it; root triages all findings
335: and revises/re-reviews material contract changes. Only then assign Sol engine
336: implementation, Terra SDK acceptance, and Luna independent validation. Run
337: focused regression/baseline demonstrations followed by engine, SDK, architecture
338: and scoped lint checks. Finish with the established Fable implementation review
339: (low effort unless the user asks otherwise). No implementation starts merely
340: because a reviewer process exited successfully; its findings must be resolved.
341: 
342: An explicit boundary transition remains future design: it would append intent
343: and a receipt-grounded cutover, identify the relevant role/name and predecessor
344: evidence, define restart versus carry, and preserve all historical rows. C6
345: does not smuggle that new replay protocol into a routine declaration edit.

=== libs/engine/src/engine/runtime_write.py ===
1: """Detached planning and one-CAS execution for an ordinary Arrival write.
2: 
3: This is deliberately narrower than the legacy runtime writer: one ordinary
4: fact, and at most the one boundary tick it causes.  It owns no SQLite handle.
5: The caller supplies a detached, already snapshot-rebuilt vertex; this module
6: turns its admission/fold result into Arrival drafts and performs one witnessed
7: append.
8: """

100: class RuntimeWriteRefused(Exception):
101:     """The runtime writer cannot establish a safe pre-append plan."""

104: class BoundaryContinuityRefused(RuntimeWriteRefused):
105:     """Current replay would consume an unlicensed historical boundary edge."""
106: 
107:     def __init__(
108:         self,
109:         *,
110:         issue: Any,
111:         basis: ReadBasis,
112:         effective_declaration: VertexFile,
113:         cause: Exception,
114:     ) -> None:
115:         super().__init__(str(cause))
116:         self.issue = issue
117:         self.basis = basis
118:         self.captured_head = basis.captured_head
119:         self.effective_declaration = effective_declaration
120:         self.tick_id = issue.tick_id
121:         self.ordinal = issue.tick_ordinal
122:         self.fact_id = issue.declaration_fact_id
123:         self.vertex = issue.vertex_name
124:         self.coordinator_phase = "prepare"
125:         self.effects = {
126:             "custody": {"attempt": "not-entered", "state": "not-attempted"}

194: class WriteCommitUnknown(Exception):
195:     """Append raised without proving that it left custody unchanged.
196: 
197:     The stable planned identities let callers reconcile this operation without
198:     treating an adapter failure as permission to append the fact again.
199:     """
200: 
201:     outcome = "unknown"
202: 
203:     def __init__(self, plan: OrdinaryWritePlan, cause: Exception) -> None:
204:         super().__init__(
205:             "ordinary append outcome is unknown; reconcile by captured head "
206:             "and planned record identities"
207:         )
208:         self.captured_head = plan.captured_head
209:         self.fact_id = plan.fact_id
210:         self.tick_id = plan.tick_id
211:         self.drafts = plan.drafts
212:         self.cause = cause

215: class BatchWriteCommitUnknown(Exception):
216:     """A batch append raised without proving that custody stayed unchanged."""
217: 
218:     outcome = "unknown"
219: 
220:     def __init__(self, plan: BatchWritePlan, cause: Exception) -> None:
221:         super().__init__(
222:             "batch append outcome is unknown; reconcile by captured head "
223:             "and every planned record identity"
224:         )
225:         self.captured_head = plan.captured_head
226:         self.items = plan.items
227:         self.fact_ids = tuple(item.fact_id for item in plan.items)
228:         self.tick_ids = plan.pending_tick_ids + tuple(
229:             item.tick_id for item in plan.items if item.tick_id is not None
230:         )
231:         self.tick_runs = plan.pending_tick_runs + tuple(
232:             item.tick_run for item in plan.items if item.tick_id is not None
233:         )
234:         self.drafts = plan.drafts
235:         self.cause = cause

238: def _close_quietly(handle: object) -> None:
239:     close = getattr(handle, "close", None)
240:     if callable(close):
241:         with suppress(Exception):
242:             close()

251:     FAILED = "failed"

254: @dataclass(frozen=True)
255: class OrdinaryWritePlan:
256:     """A fully prepared ordinary fact and optional local boundary tick."""

271:         default=(), repr=False, compare=False

280:             "ordinary append committed but projection publication failed; "

284:         self.captured_head = plan.captured_head
285:         self.fact_id = getattr(plan, "fact_id", None)
286:         self.tick_id = getattr(plan, "tick_id", None)
287:         self.items = getattr(plan, "items", None)
288:         self.drafts = plan.drafts
289:         self.cause = cause
290:         self.projection = ProjectionOutcome.FAILED
