145:                 for seq, (t, row) in enumerate(rows_of_record(record)):
146:                     table = "facts" if t == "fact" else "ticks"
147:                     yield table, row[0], ord_val, seq
148: 
149:         self._coordinate_provider = _provider
150:         # The mark the last ceremony-path reconcile verified — see
151:         # _sync_derived_state / _ceremony_persist.
152:         self._reconciled_mark: ResumeMark | None = None
153:         try:
154:             self._ensure_meta_table()
155:             self._ensure_coordinate_schema()
156:             self._ensure_fact_signature_column()
157:             self._ensure_chain_columns()
158:             self.catch_up()
159:         except BaseException:
160:             # A raise out of __init__ must leave the db reopenable: an
161:             # uncommitted write on a leaked connection locks the file.
162:             conn = self._db
163:             try:
164:                 conn.rollback()
165:             finally:
166:                 conn.close()
167:             raise
168: 
169:     @property
170:     def log_path(self) -> Path:
171:         """The arrival log this store's sqlite index derives from."""
172:         return self._log.path
173: 
174:     @property
175:     def _db(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:
176:         """The open index connection, narrowed.
177: 
178:         ``SqliteStore`` nulls ``_conn`` on close; nothing in this class runs
179:         after close, so a None here is a caller bug and refuses loudly
180:         rather than surfacing as an attribute error three frames deep.
181:         """
182:         conn = self._conn
183:         if conn is None:
184:             raise RuntimeError(f"store at {self._path} is closed")
185:         return conn
186: 
187:     # ---- the resume mark ------------------------------------------------
188: 
189:     def _read_mark(self) -> ResumeMark | None:
190:         """The persisted mark, or None when any field is absent or untyped.
191: 
192:         All three fields or nothing: a partial mark is not a position, and
193:         :meth:`engine.arrival.ArrivalLog.walk_marked` re-validates whatever
194:         this returns at the adoption site anyway.
195:         """
196:         lineage = self._meta_get(ARRIVAL_LINEAGE_KEY)
197:         offset = _as_int(self._meta_get(ARRIVAL_OFFSET_KEY))
198:         ordinal = _as_int(self._meta_get(ARRIVAL_ORDINAL_KEY))
199:         if not isinstance(lineage, str) or offset is None or ordinal is None:
200:             return None
201:         return ResumeMark(
202:             arrival_lineage=lineage, arrival_offset=offset, arrival_ordinal=ordinal
203:         )
204: 
205:     def _stamp_mark(self, mark: ResumeMark) -> None:
206:         """Stage the three mark fields — the caller owns the commit."""
207:         self._meta_set(ARRIVAL_LINEAGE_KEY, mark.arrival_lineage)
208:         self._meta_set(ARRIVAL_OFFSET_KEY, mark.arrival_offset)
209:         self._meta_set(ARRIVAL_ORDINAL_KEY, mark.arrival_ordinal)
210: 
211:     # ---- catch-up --------------------------------------------------------
212: 
213:     def catch_up(self) -> str:
214:         """Reconcile the index with the log by consuming forward.
215: 
216:         Returns what it did: ``"synced"``, ``"tailed"``, ``"built"`` or
217:         ``"empty"``. Refuses (:class:`ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported`) whenever
218:         honesty would require discarding index rows — that is projection
219:         re-derivation, the next cut's verb.
220: 
221:         Runs on every open, so "there is nothing to do" is the case that has
222:         to be cheap: it answers from ``store_meta`` reads and one stat,
223:         taking no lock at all. Everything past that fast path takes the
224:         sqlite write lock —
225:         see the comment at the escalation for why the mark is re-read inside
226:         it, and why the fast path cannot weaken that.
227:         """
228:         mark = self._read_mark()
229:         if self._log.size() == 0:
230:             if has_rows(self._db) or mark is not None:
231:                 raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
232:                     f"{self._path} carries index state but there is no "
233:                     f"arrival log at {self._log.path} — an index without its "
234:                     "log cannot be reconciled, and re-derivation cannot "
235:                     "manufacture a log: offering it here would name an "
236:                     "operation that destroys the only surviving artifact. "
237:                     "Restore the log, or open the db as a plain sqlite store"
238:                 )
239:             return "empty"
240: 
241:         if mark is None and has_rows(self._db):
242:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
243:                 f"{self._path} holds rows but no arrival resume mark — an "
244:                 "index that carries state cannot be consumed forward. Run "
245:                 "engine.arrival_projection.rederive_projections("
246:                 f"{str(self._log.path)!r}) to discard the projection and "
247:                 "rebuild it from the log, or open the db as a plain sqlite "
248:                 "store"
249:             )
250: 
251:         # NOTHING TO DO — the lock-free common case. Catch-up runs on every
252:         # open, so an index that is already current must not take the write
253:         # lock: doing so would serialize every opener behind every other.
254:         # Both halves of the conjunct are load-bearing, because both are
255:         # things this method does: nothing to CONSUME (the mark names the
256:         # log's exact end, the same predicate :meth:`_reconcile` already
257:         # short-circuits on) and nothing to RESTORE (a present ``own_lineage``
258:         # is exactly the condition under which :meth:`_restore_own_lineage`
259:         # returns without staging). Drop the marker half and the ceremony
260:         # crash window stops recovering at the next open.
261:         #
262:         # A writer landing a record between the size() read and the return
263:         # makes this "synced" momentarily stale. That is the SAME staleness
264:         # _reconcile has always accepted on the same predicate, and it is
265:         # benign for the same reason: the position is re-read on every
266:         # append, and an append whose own coordinate does not follow the
267:         # reconciled one rolls back and consumes everything forward.
268:         if (
269:             mark is not None
270:             and mark.arrival_offset == self._log.size()
271:             and self._meta_get("own_lineage") is not None
272:         ):
273:             return "synced"
274: 
275:         # Everything above is a read. Consuming is a WRITE, and the mark it
276:         # decides from must be read under the same lock that the INSERTs
277:         # take — otherwise two processes catching the same index up both
278:         # read the pre-consume mark, both replay the same records, and the
279:         # loser's INSERT collides on the primary key and is misreported as
280:         # "the index holds state the log does not account for". Two
281:         # concurrent opens of a behind store are ordinary (a merge does two
282:         # of them), so the window is escalated to BEGIN IMMEDIATE and the
283:         # mark re-read inside it. The loser then blocks, sees the winner's
284:         # stamp, and consumes nothing.
285:         #
286:         # The escalation costs nothing in the common case because the fast
287:         # path above already returned; every opener that reaches here has
288:         # real work. The arrival APPEND lock is never taken: blocking every
289:         # writer in the system to build a projection is what this design
290:         # refuses.
291:         self._db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
292:         try:
293:             mark = self._read_mark()
294:             resumed, records = self._log.walk_marked(mark)
295:             if mark is not None and resumed == 0:
296:                 raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
297:                     f"{self._path} carries an arrival resume mark the log at "
298:                     f"{self._log.path} rejects — the index cannot be consumed "
299:                     "forward. Run engine.arrival_projection."
300:                     f"rederive_projections({str(self._log.path)!r}) to discard "
301:                     "the projection and rebuild it from the log"
302:                 )
303:             last_mark: ResumeMark | None = None
304:             for record, record_mark in records:
305:                 self._index_record(record)
306:                 last_mark = record_mark
307:             self._restore_own_lineage()
308:             if last_mark is not None:
309:                 self._stamp_mark(last_mark)
310:         except BaseException:
311:             self._db.rollback()
312:             raise
313:         # Always closed, even when nothing was staged: BEGIN IMMEDIATE holds
314:         # the write lock until the transaction ends, and a return that left
315:         # it open would wedge every other writer.
316:         self._db.commit()
317:         if last_mark is None:
318:             return "synced"
319:         return "built" if mark is None else "tailed"
320: 
321:     def _restore_own_lineage(self) -> bool:
322:         """Stage the ``own_lineage`` projection when it is ABSENT.
323: 
324:         The one residue of the ceremony crash window. ``_ceremony_persist``
325:         makes the genesis record durable and stages the marker; a crash
326:         before the caller's COMMIT rolls back the ``_decl.genesis`` INSERT,
327:         the marker and the mark together. Catch-up tails the record forward
328:         and the ROW recovers itself — the marker does not, because it is
329:         not in the log's rows. Restoring it here is a pure projection
330:         restore, and it is legal automatically for the same reason building
331:         an absent index is: absent→present destroys nothing.
332: 
333:         A PRESENT marker is never touched, so this path can never overwrite
334:         an identity claim. Judging a present, disagreeing marker is
335:         :func:`engine.arrival_projection.rederive_projections`'s, where the
336:         stamp would be destructive.
337: 
338:         Returns whether it staged a write, so the caller knows whether a
339:         commit is owed on the otherwise-no-op path.
340:         """
341:         if self._meta_get("own_lineage") is not None:
342:             return False
343:         try:
344:             lineage = self._log.lineage()
345:         except GenesisRefused:
346:             return False
347:         own = licensed_own_lineage(self._db, lineage)
348:         if own is None:
349:             return False
350:         self._meta_set("own_lineage", own)
351:         return True
352: 
353:     def _index_record(self, record: dict) -> None:
354:         """Stage one record's rows into the index — verbatim, no mint
355:         machinery, no signer; signatures ride as stored in the body.
356: 
357:         What a record expands to is :func:`engine.arrival_projection.
358:         rows_of_record`'s answer and not a second copy of it: the indexer
359:         and the re-deriver disagreeing about a record is the one
360:         disagreement this design cannot tolerate.
361:         """
362:         ord_val = record["ord"]
363:         for seq, (t, row) in enumerate(rows_of_record(record)):
364:             try:
365:                 self._db.execute(
366:                     FACT_INSERT_SQL if t == "fact" else TICK_INSERT_SQL,
367:                     (*row, ord_val, seq),
368:                 )
369:             except Exception as exc:
370:                 raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
371:                     f"the index at {self._path} refuses {t} {row[0]!r} from "
372:                     f"ordinal {record['ord']} ({exc}) — it holds state the "
373:                     "log does not account for. Run engine.arrival_projection."
374:                     f"rederive_projections({str(self._log.path)!r}) to "
375:                     "discard the projection and rebuild it from the log"
376:                 ) from exc
377: 
378:     def _reconcile(self) -> ResumeMark | None:
379:         """Refuse to stamp past a durable record the index has not consumed.
380: 
381:         Same posture as the log-canonical store: a long-lived handle never
382:         reopens, so every append reconciles first. Cheap in the common case
383:         — one meta read and one stat.
384: 
385:         Returns the mark this reconcile verified or (via catch-up) stamped,
386:         so the write paths consume the reconciled position instead of
387:         re-reading ``store_meta`` — the interloper checks are DEFINED
388:         against exactly this pre-append mark. ``None`` only when the log is
389:         empty, where no append can succeed anyway.
390:         """
391:         mark = self._read_mark()
392:         if mark is not None and mark.arrival_offset == self._log.size():
393:             return mark
394:         self.catch_up()
395:         return self._read_mark()
396: 
397:     def _sync_derived_state(self) -> None:
398:         """Reconcile before mint logic reads chain state off the index.
399: 
400:         The reconciled mark is kept for :meth:`_ceremony_persist`, which
401:         runs later inside the same ceremony transaction and must pin its
402:         append to the head the CEREMONY reconciled against — not to
403:         whatever the mark says by the time it runs.
404:         """
405:         self._reconciled_mark = self._reconcile()
406: 
407:     # ---- the write path --------------------------------------------------
408: 
409:     def _custodian(self) -> str:
410:         """The label a record this store MINTS puts in its envelope observer.
411: 
412:         The destination log's own genesis observer — ruling 2 of
413:         decision:design/arrival-wire-v1-seam-triage. A tick has no author:
414:         it is the custodian's fold engine producing a record, and the
415:         envelope observer says so. The previous spelling put the tick's
416:         NAME there, which read as an authorship claim a name cannot make.
417: 
418:         Scoped to what it claims, and no further. The label is TRUE but
419:         UNVERIFIED, on purpose: tick outer signatures are deferred (ruling
420:         3), so no tick's envelope observer is ever looked up in the key
421:         registry — ``_resolve`` gates on a signature being present and
422:         every tick mint passes ``signer=None``. An unverified true claim is
423:         what wire v1 pins; a verifiable one is a later design session's.
424: 
425:         Fact records are untouched by this: their envelope observer stays
426:         the row's author, because that value SELECTS THE SIGNING KEY
427:         (``fact_signer_for`` resolves ``keys/<observer>/ed25519.key``) and
428:         re-deriving it as custody would re-key every fact signature from
429:         author to custodian — a decision explicitly not made here.
430: 
431:         Reads through the genesis memo, so a store minting many ticks pays
432:         one validation. The refusal is re-spelled rather than surfaced raw
433:         for the reason :meth:`_genesis_lineage_id` re-spells its own: this
434:         class promises an unminted log refuses appends with the mint-first
435:         message, and "the log does not exist" is that state described from
436:         the wrong end.
437:         """
438:         try:
439:             return self._log.genesis()["observer"]
440:         except GenesisRefused as exc:
441:             raise GenesisRefused(
442:                 f"{self._log.path} is empty — mint a genesis first; a "
443:                 "record's envelope observer is the log's own genesis "
444:                 "observer, so there is no custodian to name until movement "
445:                 "1 has named one"
446:             ) from exc
447: 
448:     def _write(self, sql: str, row: tuple, encode_row, is_fact: bool) -> str | None:
449:         """Stage the INSERT, make the record durable in the log, stamp, commit.
450: 
451:         The INSERT runs first, uncommitted, so a rejected row fails before a
452:         byte reaches the log and a refused write can never orphan a record.
453:         The record's body is the arrival body grammar's object for the
454:         COMMITTED read-back row — the index and the log must derive-match. The record's arrival signature comes from
455:         the injected ``fact_signer`` over the arrival commitment; the same
456:         callable, a different digest, and the composing layer's
457:         domain-separation prefix already binds both to this store family.
458: 
459:         A record another writer lands in the log between the reconcile and
460:         the append would otherwise be skipped forever: our record arrives
461:         one ordinal later and the stamped mark would claim consumption it
462:         never performed. The append's own coordinate is the tell — when it
463:         is not the reconciled ordinal plus one, the staged INSERT rolls
464:         back (our record is already durable in the log) and catch-up
465:         consumes everything forward instead, the interloper included. The
466:         returned signature is the committed row's either way: catch-up
467:         re-inserts the identical row verbatim from the record's body.
468:         """
469:         consumed = self._reconcile()
470:         # Grammar pre-flight on the ASSEMBLED row, NOT dead work: sqlite's
471:         # column affinity coerces (e.g. a string ts commits as REAL), so the
472:         # committed-row encode below would ACCEPT a row the grammar refuses
473:         # — this is the gate that fails at the append site, where it is
474:         # attributable, instead of laundering the value.
475:         encode_row(row)
476:         try:
477:             table = "facts" if is_fact else "ticks"
478:             expected_ordinal = 0 if consumed is None else consumed.arrival_ordinal + 1
479:             staged_row = (*row, expected_ordinal, 0)
480:             self._db.execute(sql, staged_row)
481:             committed_row = self._committed_full_row(
482:                 table, row[0]
483:             )
484:             sig_col_idx = (
485:                 FACT_COLUMN_INDEX if is_fact else TICK_COLUMN_INDEX
486:             )["signature"]
487:             committed = committed_row[sig_col_idx]
488:             body = encode_row(committed_row[:-2])
489:             record, mark = self._log.append_marked(
490:                 "fact" if is_fact else "tick",
491:                 body,
492:                 observer=committed_row[3] if is_fact else self._custodian(),
493:                 origin=committed_row[4],
494:                 at=committed_row[2],
495:                 signer=self._fact_signer if is_fact else None,
496:             )
497:             # consumed is None on one REACHABLE interleave: the log was
498:             # empty at reconcile time and another process minted the
499:             # genesis inside the reconcile->append gap. Legal interleave is
500:             # refuse-or-consume, never crash — so a missing reconciled mark
501:             # takes the same gap path as a stale one: roll back and consume
502:             # everything forward, the fresh genesis included.
503:             if consumed is None or record["ord"] != consumed.arrival_ordinal + 1:
504:                 self._db.rollback()
505:                 self.catch_up()
506:                 return committed
507:             self._stamp_mark(mark)
508:             self._db.commit()
509:             return committed
510:         except BaseException:
511:             self._db.rollback()
512:             raise
513: 
514: 
515:     def _allocate_ceremony_coordinates(
516:         self, conn: Any, count: int
517:     ) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
518:         consumed = self._reconciled_mark
519:         expected_ordinal = 0 if consumed is None else consumed.arrival_ordinal + 1
520:         return [(expected_ordinal, idx) for idx in range(count)]
521: 
522:     def _write_fact_row(self, row: tuple) -> str | None:
523:         return self._write(FACT_INSERT_SQL, row, body_of_fact_row, True)
524: 
525:     def _write_tick_row(self, row: tuple) -> str | None:
526:         return self._write(TICK_INSERT_SQL, row, body_of_tick_row, False)
527: 
528:     def _ceremony_persist(self, rows: list[tuple]) -> None:
529:         """Make a declaration ceremony canonical: ONE arrival record, stamped.
530: 
531:         Runs inside the ceremony's open transaction after every check
532:         passed, immediately before the caller's COMMIT. One row rides as a
533:         ``fact`` record; several as one ``batch`` record — one record is the
534:         log's atomicity unit, so recovery can never expose a partial
535:         ceremony.
536: 
537:         ``following`` pins the append to the FULL head the ceremony
538:         reconciled against (:meth:`_pinned_head`): a record another writer
539:         lands mid-ceremony REFUSES the append before any byte is written
540:         (``StaleHead``, an ``AppendRejected``), the caller's transaction
541:         rolls back with the log byte-identical, and the ceremony is simply
542:         retryable — unlike :meth:`_write`, this path cannot resolve a gap
543:         after the fact, because the record it would have made durable is the
544:         ceremony itself.
545:         """
546:         persisted_rows = [
547:             r[:-2] if len(r) == len(FACT_ALL_COLUMNS) else r for r in rows
548:         ]
549:         if len(persisted_rows) > 1:
550:             k, body = "batch", body_of_batch(persisted_rows)
551:         else:
552:             k, body = "fact", body_of_fact_row(persisted_rows[0])
553:         _, mark = self._log.append_marked(
554:             k,
555:             body,
556:             observer=rows[0][3],
557:             origin=rows[0][4],
558:             at=rows[0][2],
559:             signer=self._fact_signer,
560:             following=self._pinned_head(self._reconciled_mark),
561:         )
562:         self._stamp_mark(mark)
563: 
564:     def _pinned_head(self, consumed: ResumeMark | None) -> Head | None:
565:         """The full head a ceremony reconciled against, for the append's pin.
566: 
567:         The resume mark carries a coordinate and a seek hint but no record
568:         hash, and the compare-and-swap now pins all three head fields — so
569:         the record the mark names is read back to supply the third.
570:         :meth:`engine.arrival.ArrivalLog.anchor` is a seek to the mark's own
571:         offset with the anchor checks applied, not a walk, so completing the
572:         pin costs one read rather than a pass over the log.
573: 
574:         A mark the log will not vouch for is a REFUSAL, never a quiet fall
575:         back to an unpinned append. Dropping the pin because it could not be
576:         completed is precisely the silent weakening the full-head compare
577:         exists to prevent, and it would turn a store whose index disagrees
578:         with its log into one that appends onto the disagreement.
579:         """
580:         if consumed is None:
581:             return None
582:         anchor = self._log.anchor(consumed)
583:         if anchor is None:
584:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
585:                 f"{self._path} reconciled against arrival ordinal "
586:                 f"{consumed.arrival_ordinal} of lineage "
587:                 f"{consumed.arrival_lineage}, but {self._log.path} does not "
588:                 "vouch for a record there — the index's mark and the log "
589:                 "disagree, and appending under an uncompletable pin would "
590:                 "write onto that disagreement. Run "
591:                 "engine.arrival_projection.rederive_projections("
592:                 f"{str(self._log.path)!r})"
593:             )
594:         return Head(
595:             lineage=anchor["lin"],
596:             ordinal=anchor["ord"],
597:             record_hash=anchor["rh"],
598:         )
599: 
600:     def _declaration_head_in_txn(
601:         self, conn: Any, lineage_id: str
602:     ) -> tuple[int, str] | None:
603:         """The ``(record_ordinal, id)`` of the newest self-lineage declaration.
604: 
605:         The CAS token names the axis this store actually accretes on. Its
606:         first coordinate is the ARRIVAL ordinal of the log record whose
607:         expansion carries the row — so the rows of one batch record (a
608:         multi-change edit ceremony) share an ordinal and the tie-break falls
609:         to the fact id, which is the one place this axis is observably not
610:         the index's rowid.
611: 
612:         The predicate is the base class's, verbatim: own genesis
613:         participates, and a foreign ``_decl.*`` row is excluded by the
614:         lineage its payload stamps. Only the coordinate changed.
615: 
616:         The log answers, because the log is the store. No ordinal column, no
617:         side table: a second place the coordinate is written is a second
618:         thing that can disagree with the log, and the indexer/re-deriver
619:         disagreement is the one this family does not tolerate. The cost is
620:         one forward walk per read — twice per edit ceremony, and ceremonies
621:         are rare.
622: 
623:         Bounded at the reconciled position rather than at end-of-file. The
624:         ceremony reads this INSIDE its transaction, having reconciled
625:         against a specific prefix; a record another writer lands mid-ceremony
626:         is :meth:`_ceremony_persist`'s ``following`` refusal to judge, not
627:         this read's, and the bound also keeps a walk from reaching a tail
628:         that is mid-append.
629:         """
630:         mark = self._read_mark()
631:         if mark is None:
632:             return None
633:         best: tuple[int, str] | None = None
634:         for record in self._log.walk():
635:             ordinal = record["ord"]
636:             if ordinal > mark.arrival_ordinal:
637:                 break
638:             for kind_of_row, row in rows_of_record(record):
639:                 if kind_of_row != "fact":
640:                     continue
641:                 fact_id, kind, payload_text = row[0], row[1], row[5]
642:                 if not kind.startswith("_decl."):
643:                     continue
644:                 if fact_id == lineage_id:
645:                     pass  # own genesis participates
646:                 else:
647:                     try:
648:                         payload = json.loads(payload_text)
649:                     except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError):
650:                         continue
651:                     if payload.get("lineage") != lineage_id:
652:                         continue
653:                 candidate = (ordinal, fact_id)
654:                 if best is None or candidate > best:
655:                     best = candidate
656:         return best
657: 
658:     # ---- ceremonies -------------------------------------------------------
659: 
660:     def _genesis_lineage_id(self) -> str:
661:         """The declaration genesis row projects the ARRIVAL lineage.
662: 
663:         ``store_meta.own_lineage`` is stamped with this same value by the
664:         base ceremony, so identity is written from arrival, read from
665:         sqlite, and re-derivable from the log with every projection gone —
666:         the strongest assertion in the cut's gate. The row id being the
667:         lineage id preserves the legacy invariant the CAS head-tracking
668:         keys off.
669:         """
670:         try:
671:             return self._log.lineage()
672:         except GenesisRefused as exc:
673:             raise GenesisRefused(
674:                 f"{self._log.path} has no arrival genesis — the declaration "
675:                 "absorb is movement 2; mint the arrival genesis (movement 1, "
676:                 "with the founding key) first"
677:             ) from exc
678: 
679:     def _genesis_payload(self, protocol: int, documents: list) -> dict[str, Any]:
680:         """``{protocol, documents}`` — the era pins dissolve.
681: 
682:         The old cursor pin's claim ("everything before me predates
683:         historization") is ``ord < N``, structural under the dense ordinal;
684:         the old chain-head pin was a tick-row hash, a projection artifact a
685:         signed immutable record must not rest an integrity claim on
686:         (arrival law 1). A later cut that wants a tick anchor names an
687:         arrival coordinate, not a row hash.
688:         """
689:         return {"protocol": protocol, "documents": list(documents)}
690: 
691:     def adopt_lineage(self, lineage_id: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
692:         """Refused: an arrival store's identity is structural at ordinal 0.
693: 
694:         There is nothing to adopt — the log's genesis IS the identity, and
695:         foreign genesis records cannot exist above ordinal 0. Restamping the
696:         ``own_lineage`` projection marker from the log is projection repair,
697:         which :meth:`catch_up` does when the marker is absent and
698:         :func:`engine.arrival_projection.rederive_projections` does when it
699:         re-derives everything; deleting this ceremony would break the legacy
700:         modes that still need it. Refusal keeps both doors open.
701:         """
702:         raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
703:             "adopt is not a ceremony an arrival-canonical store has: "
704:             "identity is the arrival genesis at ordinal 0, structurally — "
705:             "there is nothing to choose between. If the own_lineage marker "
706:             "is missing, reopening the store restores it from the log "
707:             "(catch-up), and engine.arrival_projection.rederive_projections "
708:             "restores it alongside every other projection row."
709:         )
710: 
711:     def reanchor(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any):  # noqa: D102
712:         # PERMANENT, not deferred (cut B). The queued log-rewrite ceremony is
713:         # RETIRED: rewriting a log is not an operation in this model, and the
714:         # rowid-identity property the projections rest on is exactly what a
715:         # rewrite would destroy. The one case that could break re-derivation
716:         # is a truncated or rewritten log, and this is the method that would
717:         # have made it reachable.
718:         raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
719:             "reanchor is not an operation an arrival-canonical store has, and "
720:             "it is not a later slice's either: it would rewrite index rows "
721:             "while the arrival log kept the originals, so the index would "
722:             "stop being a function of the log. Rewriting a log is not an "
723:             "operation in this model."
724:         )
725: 
726: 
727: def ensure_arrival_index(canonical: Path) -> Path:
728:     """Materialize — and catch up — the sqlite index for an arrival log.
729: 
730:     The fresh-clone case: the log is the store, the derived ``.db`` is not
731:     tracked, so the first read finds no index. Opening an
732:     :class:`ArrivalStore` runs catch-up (an absent index builds forward from
733:     ordinal 0); closing immediately leaves no handle behind. A no-op when
734:     the log is missing or the index is already current
735:     (:func:`engine.jsonl_store._stamped_offset_current`, keyed on the
736:     arrival cursor).
737:     """
738:     canonical = Path(canonical)
739:     index = index_path_for(canonical)
740:     if not canonical.exists():
741:         return index
742:     if index.exists() and _stamped_offset_current(index, canonical, ARRIVAL_OFFSET_KEY):
743:         return index
744:     store: ArrivalStore[Any] = ArrivalStore(
745:         path=index,
746:         log_path=canonical,
747:         serialize=lambda d: d,
748:         deserialize=lambda d: d,
749:     )
750:     store.close()
751:     return index

=== docs/scratch/arrival-break/reviews/fresh-epoch-fable-2026-09-18/findings.md ===
1: **ACCEPT.** I found no P1 in the frozen source. The P2s below should land before the real cutover, and the first missing-context item is an unverified risk that becomes P1 if it holds. This was a static review; no tests were run.
2: 
3: Fresh filtering is consistent across the execution consumers I could see: hydration, pending boundaries, source cadence, C6 owned ticks, `read_state`, and aggregate per-member floors. Global dedup, the tick-id collision check, signed-era detection, `prev_hash`, and window hashes all read full history. The physical-vs-projection comparison catches an invented marker, a stripped marker, and a moved ordinal or sequence. Own-lineage selection cannot be influenced by foreign genesis rows. Recovery rejects both a field-only and a field-plus-payload epoch tamper, the second through FACT signature verification.
4: 
5: ## P2
6: 
7: 1. **Strict-mode hydration widened to ticks with negative `ts` — `runtime_write.py:949-964,1008`.**
8:    - **Trigger:** a strict store holding an own-origin tick with `ts < 0`.
9:    - **Consequence:** that tick now resets the loop on rehydrate, where previously `TickRequest()` with `since=0` excluded it from hydration. This is a behaviour change in the default mode. It contradicts the accepted C6 note that hydration selection "must not be widened accidentally" (`consistency-c6…md:159-162`).
10:    - The same diff changes `plan_ordinary_write:1119` and `capture._ticks` to read full tick history. That fixes a latent wrong-`prev_hash` and chain break when the latest physical tick has negative `ts`, so I would keep both changes.
11:    - **Fix:** record the decision in the C6 design and add a strict regression test covering a negative-`ts` owned tick for both the hydrate reset and the chain predecessor. The only current coverage is the fresh-mode test.
12: 
13: 2. **Acceptance item 7 is not actually tested — `tests/test_arrival_migration_boundary.py:612-618`.**
14:    - **Trigger:** the `epoch-downgrade` mutation rewrites the payload but leaves the intent root field `"runtime_epoch": "fresh"`. It is therefore caught by the payload-equality check at `arrival_adoption.py:762-770`, not by signature verification.
15:    - **Consequence:** a self-consistent downgrade (root field set to `strict` plus a protocol-1 payload) is never exercised. The code does refuse it, because the FACT digest no longer verifies, but no test proves that.
16:    - **Fix:** add a mutation that also sets the root field to `strict`, and the reverse case (strict to fresh) on the strict parameterisation.
17:    - **Related:** `_plan_from_intent:646` takes the mode from the unsigned intent field and cross-checks it against the draft. The design text and ADOPTION.md say the mode is "derived from the draft". Deriving it from the draft payload would remove the redundant field.
18: 
19: 3. **Rehearsal signature evidence no longer reads custody bytes — `scripts/arrival_rehearsal.py:209-239`.**
20:    - **Trigger:** `_verify_rehearsal_emit` now verifies the in-memory `commit.records` instead of `ledger.read(...)`.
21:    - **Consequence:** `signed_fact_and_arrival: true` attests the returned Commit object rather than the physical row.
22:    - **Fix:** after matching the record, call `ledger.read(record["ord"])`, require it to equal the commit record, and verify the signatures on the row that was read.
23: 
24: 4. **Rehearsal epoch and history assertions are one-sided — `scripts/arrival_rehearsal.py:447-454,430-434`.**
25:    - **Trigger:** the script asserts the fresh epoch only. It never checks `adopted.runtime_epoch == args.runtime_epoch`, that a strict run reports `{"mode": "strict", "anchor_ordinal": None}`, or that a fresh run's sections are at their declared initial values (only a hash is recorded).
26:    - **Consequence:** the claim "history preserved" is recorded (`fact_total`, `tick_total`) but never compared against the inventory counts less dropped units.
27:    - **Fix:** add these assertions to the script.
28: 
29: ## Nonblocking
30: 
31: - **Low-level downgrade bypass:** `hydrate_arrival_candidate` and `runtime_epoch_from_anchor` accept `wire_record=None` and return strict without any physical check (`declaration.py:150-155`, `runtime_write.py:1041-1057`). A projection stripped of its marker therefore passes on this API. Supported paths always supply the wire record. Consider requiring it, or naming the unverified variant.
32: - **Read cost:** `OpenedRead.runtime_epoch()` (`arrival_consumer.py:96-106`) calls `ledger.read(A)`, which is a verified walk from ordinal 0 (`arrival.py:1207-1220`). On a migrated store A sits at the end of history, so every `read_state`, every aggregate member capture (`arrival_aggregate.py:256`), and every `capture_runtime` pays O(history).
33:    - Aggregate summary and timeline reads pay this too, although they do not need the epoch.
34:    - `read_state` computes it twice for an aggregate root.
35:    - It cannot be skipped for strict stores without reopening the downgrade hole, so compute it lazily for aggregates and cache it by anchor `record_hash`.
36: - **Adapter contract tightened:** `runtime_epoch_from_anchor` is called with a wire record even for strict stores, so `read_state` now needs the adapter to supply the exact `payload_text` and `signature` on `Fact`. This is not recorded on `QuerySnapshot`.
37: - **Payload built in two places:** `_anchor_payload` and `build_declaration_anchor_draft` build the payload separately, and it is identical today. Several sites also hard-code protocol `1`, and the docstring at `document.py:150` is now stale. Share one builder and one named base-protocol constant.
38: - **Narrower protocol acceptance:** `_runtime_epoch_payload` now refuses protocol 0, negative, float, or string values that the old `> VERSION` check accepted. This is the fail-closed direction, but it changes behaviour for legacy resolution.
39: - **Doc wording:** "first post-A event starts period context" holds for pending planning only. Hydration never seeds `_vertex_period_start` from facts, so a first post-A boundary gets `since` equal to its own `ts`. This is existing behaviour and only the wording is off.
40: 
41: ## Missing context
42: 
43: - **Possible P1:** grep every use of `DECLARATION_PROTOCOL_VERSION`, including `arrival_store._genesis_payload`, the app's absorb command, `migrate`, the `store` lib, and the Go oracle vectors. Any emitter that stamps the constant now writes protocol 2 without the marker, and every reader will refuse that payload.
44: - Check other callers of `analyze_boundary_continuity`, `_build_effective_arrival_candidate` (which now returns 8 values instead of 6), and `RuntimeCapture.facts` and `.ticks` in `sdk/declare.py` and `sdk/source.py`.
45: - Confirm `rows_of_body` yields tuples, since the comparison at `declaration.py:168` uses tuple equality.
46: - Check how `normalize_exception` maps `DeclarationResolutionError` and `UnsupportedProtocol`, which `open_read` can now raise through `validate_arrival_declaration_anchor`.
47: - Confirm the CLI and `open_vertex` paths over a fresh store reach the legacy resolver's refusal rather than doing a full-history `Vertex.replay`.

+        "before", "note", 1.0, "kyle", "", {"amount": 3}, 1, 0,
+        '{"amount":3}',
+    )
+    negative_tick = Tick(
+        id="negative-tick", name="note", ts=-1.0, since=None,
+        origin="effective", payload={"total": 3}, arrival_ordinal=2,
+        arrival_seq=0, payload_text='{"total":3}', prev_hash=None,
+        window_start="", fact_cursor="before", window_hash="prior-window",
+        signature=None,
+    )
+    snapshot = Snapshot(
+        head, facts=(before,), ticks=(negative_tick,), documents=documents,
+    )
+    snapshot._facts = (snapshot.declaration_anchor.genesis, before)
+    basis = ReadBasis(head.lineage, head, head, "test-view")
+    locator = parse_vertex('name "locator"\nloops { note { fold { count "inc" } } }\n')
+
+    candidate = hydrate_arrival_candidate(snapshot, basis, locator)
+    assert candidate.state("note") == {"total": 3}
+    plan = plan_ordinary_write(
+        snapshot, basis, candidate,
+        AtomFact("note", 2.0, {"amount": 4}, observer="kyle"),
+        grant=None, credentials=WriteCredentials(), custodian="kyle",
+        fact_id="after",
+    )
+    expected_predecessor = tick_row_hash((
+        negative_tick.id, negative_tick.name, negative_tick.ts,
+        negative_tick.since, negative_tick.origin, negative_tick.payload_text,
+        negative_tick.prev_hash, negative_tick.window_start,
+        negative_tick.fact_cursor, negative_tick.window_hash,
+        negative_tick.signature,
+    ))
+    assert plan.drafts[1].body["prev_hash"] == expected_predecessor
